
<!-- b001.report.md -->
# b001 report

All six routes had no on-file prose (approach, itinerary, descent, breakdown all null), so all text comes from online research and the pins were used only to check direction of travel.

- ca_east_arete_3 (Mt. Russell): 2/2 written. Drive to Whitney Portal; leg up the North Fork of Lone Pine Creek to Upper Boy Scout Lake, the Russell-Carillon saddle, then the East Arete. Defect: the summit pin's elevation is 14,009 ft, but Mount Russell is 14,088 ft. That is 79 ft low, so the pin may sit on the lower east summit or the elevation is wrong.
- ca_north_arete_4 (Bear Creek Spire): 2/2 written. Drive Rock Creek Road to Mosquito Flat; leg via Little Lakes Valley, Gem Lakes, toward Dade Lake. Minor: the summit pin's elevation is 13,675 ft, but the peak is usually given as 13,713 to 13,720 ft. The summit distMi of 6.1 is a little long against the usual 4.5 to 5 miles to the base, though that may be acceptable to the top.
- ca_north_arete_6 (Matterhorn Peak): 2/2 written. Drive to Mono Village; leg up the Horse Creek Trail and use trail to the bench lake, the glacier, the East Couloir and the arete. Minor: the summit pin's elevation is 12,299 ft, but the peak is 12,279 ft. The approach detail beyond the bench lake is supported by two sources but is generalized.
- ca_north_ridge_4 (Lone Pine Peak): 1/1 (drive-only). The drive to the Meysan Lakes Trailhead parking matches the pin, which is about 0.5 mile east of and below Whitney Portal, at 7,946 ft.
- ca_northwest_chute (Tower Peak): 2/2 written. Drive CA-108 to Leavitt Meadows; leg via the West Walker River Trail, Upper Piute Meadows, Tower Lake, Tower Pass and the NW Chute. Note: the route is about 15 to 17 miles one way and the pin says 15.7, which is consistent. Tower Lake and Tower Pass have no pins, so the single leg is long.
- ca_south_lake_peaks_traverse (Hurd Peak): 1/1 (drive-only). The drive from Bishop on CA-168 and South Lake Road is written. I did not include the recent South Lake Road reconstruction or overnight-parking restrictions because they are dated status, but whoever runs this should know about them.

No source is named in any string.

<!-- b002.report.md -->
# b002 report

All six routes had empty on-file prose except Avalanche Gulch, so the text comes from online research (land-manager, trail and route pages). No sources are named in any string.

- **ca_southeast_face_aka_the_waterfall_route**: 2 of 2 written (the North Lake drive, then the Piute Pass Trail to below Loch Leven, cross-country NW, and the left crack). Defect (minor): the summit pin elevation is 13,190 ft, while Mt. Emerson is usually given as 13,204 ft.
- **ca_southwest_slope_and_northwest_face**: 2 of 2 written. Defects: (1) the summit `distMi` is 7, but the standard approach is 5.4 mi to Piute Pass plus about 3.2 mi cross-country to the upper Humphreys Lake before the climb starts, about 10 mi in total. (2) The summit pin elevation is 13,912 ft. Mt. Humphreys is 13,986 ft, so the pin is about 74 ft low.
- **ca_the_mountaineer_s_route**: 2 of 2 written. Defects (minor): the summit pin is 14,474 ft, but Whitney is 14,505 ft (or 14,494 on the older survey). Summit `distMi` is 4, but the route is usually given as about 5.5 to 6 mi one way.
- **ca_west_face_11**: 2 of 2 written. The drive goes to the Symmes Creek hiker trailhead, which matches the pin (36.727, -118.279, 6,328 ft). I deliberately left out the old seasonal bighorn-sheep closure of the Williamson Bowl because sources say it was lifted around 2011. Defect (minor): the summit pin is 14,356 ft, while Williamson is usually 14,375 ft.
- **ca_mt_shasta_avalanche_gulch** (drive-only): 1 of 1 written (I-5 to Lake St to Everitt Memorial Hwy, plus permits). It agrees with the on-file approach.
- **ca_saber_ridge_traverse_south_to_north** (drive-only): 1 of 1 written (Generals Hwy to Crescent Meadow Road). I left out the summer weekend shuttle-only restriction because it varies from year to year. I did not verify that Crescent Meadow / the High Sierra Trail is the right trailhead for Saber Ridge; it fits the Tamarack Lake area path, but the row has no prose to confirm it.

<!-- b003.report.md -->
# b003 report

All six rows have no on-file prose (approach, itinerary, descent and the rest are null), so every entry comes from online research. 10 entries written, none left blank.

- **ca_northwest_arete_2** (Mt Clark): 2 of 2 written. The drive goes to Happy Isles. Private cars cannot reach it, so the text sends the climber to the Curry Village trailhead parking and then a walk or the shuttle. The leg runs Mist Trail/JMT, then Little Yosemite Valley, then the Merced Lake trail past Bunnell Point, then southeast cross-country up a drainage to the NW ridge. Defect: the summit pin's elevation is 11,466 ft. Mt Clark is about 11,522 ft, so the pin is about 56 ft low, which may mean it sits slightly off the summit.
- **ca_northwest_buttress_2** (Tenaya Peak): 2 of 2 written. The pin (NE-shore pullout) agrees with the published parking at the east/northeast end of Tenaya Lake, and the summit lies SE of it, which is consistent with heading south. No defect found.
- **ca_southeast_buttress_2** (Cathedral Peak): 2 of 2 written. **Defect:** the trailhead pin (37.87317, -119.38282) is at the old roadside start of the Cathedral Lakes trail, about 0.5 mi west of the visitor center. In 2022 NPS moved the trailhead and its parking to a lot next to the Tuolumne Meadows Visitor Center, connected to the trail by a new connector, and roadside parking is now banned. The drive text sends people to the new lot. The pin should probably move, and distMi 2.7 leaves out the roughly 0.75 mi connector. Possible minor issue: the summit pin's elevation is 10,805 ft, while Cathedral Peak is about 10,911 ft.
- **ca_tenaya_matthes_cathedral_traverse**: drive-only, index 0 written (Tenaya Lake NE-end parking).
- **ca_tuolumne_alpine_triple_crown**: drive-only, index 0 written (Saddlebag Lake via Hwy 120 and Saddlebag Lake Road).
- **ca_west_ridge_5** (Mt Conness): 2 of 2 written. The leg follows the standard Saddlebag approach: dam, then the Carnegie station, then Alpine Lake, then the East Ridge, then the summit plateau. From there it drops down the cairned sandy gully to the base of the West Ridge and climbs back to the summit, so the approach goes over the peak first. Note: the two Saddlebag trailhead pins in this batch (here and in the triple_crown row) sit about 10 m apart but give elevations of 10,116 ft and 10,040 ft (the lake is about 10,087 ft). The 76 ft gap means at least one elevation is off.

<!-- b004.report.md -->
# b004 report

- co_a_walk_in_the_park (drive-only, 1 wp): 1 written (drive to Glacier Gorge TH via Bear Lake Road). Row has only the trailhead pin and no prose.
- co_arapaho_peaks_traverse (legs, 2 wp): 2 written. DEFECTS: the route is a traverse to North Arapaho (route_breakdown, descent_text) but the last pin is South Arapaho, so North Arapaho has no pin. The approach puts South Arapaho about 4.3 mi from the trailhead, but the pin's distMi is 4.1 (minor). The approach's wording "the saddle between the peaks' southeast shoulder" is garbled; my leg says "the saddle on the peak's southeast shoulder".
- co_blitzen_ridge (drive-only, 1 wp): 1 written (Fall River Entrance, then Old Fall River Road, then Lawn Lake TH). The Lawn Lake TH pin has a null elev.
- co_east_ridge_the_citadel_clear_creek_co (drive-only, 1 wp): 1 written (I-70 Exit 218 to Herman Gulch TH).
- co_glacier_gorge_traverse (drive-only, 1 wp): 1 written (same drive as A Walk In The Park). The Glacier Gorge TH pin has a null elev, but co_a_walk_in_the_park's pin for the same trailhead, about 15 m away, stores 9169 ft.
- co_longs_peak_keyhole_route (legs, 2 wp): 2 written (the CO 7 / Longs Peak Road drive and the standard Keyhole line). The row has no on-file prose at all (approach, descent and breakdown are all null), so the leg text comes from published descriptions of the standard route. The summit pin is 14,251 ft against the commonly cited 14,255/14,259 ft (minor).

<!-- b005.report.md -->
# b005 report

All six routes have 2 waypoints (trailhead, summit). 12 of 12 entries written, none left blank.

- co_pyramid_peak_northeast_ridge (2/2): drive and leg from on-file prose plus land-manager/route sources. DEFECT: the summit pin's elev is 13,800 ft, but Pyramid Peak is about 14,018 ft. The row's own route_breakdown says "Above about 13,900 ft the angle eases to the summit", which contradicts the pin. The coordinate itself sits on the summit.
- co_south_ramp (2/2): this row has no prose, so the text comes from research. Route: Bear Lake connector, Glacier Gorge trail, Mills Lake, Jewel Lake, Black Lake, then the South Ramp. Note: the summit pin's distMi is 5.5, but Bear Lake to Black Lake alone is about 4.9 mi before the roughly 2,000 ft climb to Arrowhead. The figure looks somewhat short, though not clearly wrong.
- co_spiral_route (2/2): no prose, so researched. Route: Fern Lake trail to the Joe Mills/Notchtop saddle, Lake Helene, the tarn below the south ridge. Pins are consistent: the summit is NW of Bear Lake.
- co_wetterhorn_peak_southeast_ridge (2/2): no prose, so researched. The drive goes to the upper (4WD) Matterhorn Creek trailhead, matching the pin elev of 10,872 ft, and mentions the lower lot for 2WD cars. DEFECT: the summit pin's elev is 13,942 ft, but Wetterhorn is about 14,015 ft. The coordinate is on the summit.
- co_wham_ridge (2/2): no prose, so researched. Route: Molas Trail lot, Colorado Trail to the Animas, Elk Creek, beaver ponds, Vestal Creek climbers' trail, Vestal Basin. I left out a mileage for the Vestal Creek section because sources disagree (about 2.5 to 5 mi). Pins look consistent.
- co_wilson_peak_southwest_ridge (2/2): from on-file prose plus researched road numbers (Silver Pick Road, FS 622, FS 645, and the Big Bear Creek ford). DEFECT: the summit pin's elev is 13,939 ft, but Wilson Peak is about 14,017 ft. The row's own breakdown puts the false summit at about 13,900 ft, just 39 ft below the stored summit. The coordinate is on the summit.

Pattern: all three 14er summit pins (Pyramid, Wetterhorn, Wilson) store elevations 70 to 220 ft low while their coordinates are correct. That looks like a smoothed-DEM lookup and is probably a class beyond this batch.

<!-- b006.report.md -->
# b006 report

All 6 routes have 2 waypoints (trailhead, summit). 12 of 12 entries are written and none are blank. Five routes had no on-file prose, so their text comes from online route and drive descriptions. Diamond Peak's text comes from its own `approach` and `route_breakdown`.

- **co_castle_peak_northeast_ridge** (2/2). Defect: the summit pin `elev` is **14,448**. Castle Peak is about 14,265-14,279 ft, so 14,448 is wrong by about 170 ft. The trailhead pin is at the end of the pavement, which matches the drive. distMi 6 is close to the one-way distance through Montezuma Basin from the paved end (about 6.75 mi).
- **co_grays_peak_north_slopes** (2/2). No defect. The summit elev of 14,256 is slightly below the published 14,270 ft.
- **co_huron_peak_northwest_slopes** (2/2). Defect: both pins have **elev null**. The trailhead pin (38.960, -106.461) is about 2 mi SSW of Winfield. That matches the 4WD South Winfield / Lake Ann trailhead, which is what the drive describes, and distMi 2.9 fits it.
- **co_mount_elbert_northeast_ridge** (2/2). Minor: the summit elev is 14,464, but Elbert is 14,433 ft (NGVD) or 14,440 ft (NAVD88). It is about 25-30 ft high.
- **id_diamond_peak_east_ridge** (2/2). Defect: the summit pin elev is **12,155**, but the row's own route_breakdown says **12,197 ft**. Minor: the trailhead pin is at 8,207 ft and the approach prose says "about 8,000 ft".
- **id_southwest_ridge** (Borah, 2/2). Defect: the summit pin elev is **12,577**. Borah Peak is 12,662 ft, so it is about 85 ft low. The trailhead pin (7,392 ft) is consistent.

<!-- b007.report.md -->
# b007 report

All 6 routes written; 13 of 13 entries filled. None were left blank.

## id_thompson_peak_south_couloir (2/2)
- Drive: ID-75 south from Stanley, Redfish Lake Road, backpackers' lot. Leg: condensed from the route's own approach and route_breakdown.
- DEFECT: Thompson Peak summit pin elev 10,530 ft. The peak is 10,751 ft, so the pin is about 220 ft low. The lat/lng looks right; the elevation is wrong.

## mt_phantom_creek_trail (2/2)
- There is no on-file prose at all: approach, descent, breakdown and climbing_route are all null. Both entries come from research: MT-78, MT-419, Fishtail, West Rosebud Road, then West Rosebud Trail #19, Phantom Creek Trail #17, Froze-to-Death Plateau, the Tempest-Granite saddle, and the east ridge.
- DEFECT: the summit pin's distMi is 8.5. Published one-way distances for this approach run about 11 to 12 mi (about 3 mi to the Phantom Creek turn, plus switchbacks, plus about 5 mi across the plateau, plus the saddle and ridge).
- DEFECT: the summit pin elev is 12,727 ft. Granite Peak is 12,799 ft, so the pin is about 70 ft low.
- Naming: the route is called "Phantom Creek Trail", but what it climbs is the standard east ridge of Granite (4th to low 5th). That is worth checking against the grade "4th".
- My drive text says "follow the signs for Mystic Lake" without a mileage. The sources disagree on the West Rosebud Road distance (about 17 mi in one, 7 + 14.4 mi in another).

## mt_sacagawea_direct (2/2)
- Drive: MT-86 (Bridger Canyon Road), then Fairy Lake Road (FS 74). Leg: from the route's own approach and route_breakdown.
- Minor: the summit pin elev is 9,578 ft. Sacagawea Peak is about 9,665 ft, so the pin is about 90 ft low.
- Note: the approach says the trail goes "west". The summit pin is SSW of the trailhead. That is consistent enough, since the trail goes up to the divide and then the peak is south of the pass.

## or_hurwal_divide_traverse (3/3)
- Drive: Joseph, Airport Lane / Hurricane Creek Road, County Road 521 / FR 8205.
- DEFECT: the on-file approach says the Thorp Creek trail leaves the Hurricane Creek Trail "on the right". A land-area hiking guide says the unmarked turnoff is on the LEFT, which also fits the pins: you head upstream (south) and Sacajawea is east of the creek. I left out the side in the leg text. The `approach` column should be corrected.
- Leg 1 adds "up Thorp Creek to the east ridge, then the ridge to the summit", which researched sources support. The breakdown says only "fell fields and talus to the ridge".
- The pins stop at the Matterhorn. The traverse continues along the Hurwal Divide to Chief Joseph Mountain, and there are no pins for that or for the descent. That isn't a defect, just partial coverage.
- Minor: Sacajawea pin 9,810 ft (peak about 9,838) and Matterhorn pin 9,804 ft (peak about 9,826). Both are slightly low.

## or_diamond_peak_south_ridge (2/2)
- Drive: OR-58, FR 60, FR 6010 (rough, high clearance). Leg: from the route's own approach and route_breakdown.
- DEFECT: the summit pin elev is 8,571 ft. Diamond Peak is 8,744 ft, so it is about 170 ft low. That is suspiciously close to the false summit, so check whether the pin sits on the false summit or just has a bad elevation.

## or_middle_sister_north_ridge_hayden_glacier (2/2)
- There is no on-file prose. Both entries come from research:
  - Drive: OR-242 to FR 15, stay left at the Y, Pole Creek road end.
  - Leg: Pole Creek Trail 1.5 mi, Green Lakes Trail 0.7 mi, Camp Lake Trail 1.9 mi to the climbers' path, then the Hayden Glacier to the North/Middle Sister col and the north ridge.
- The trail mileages come from a single trip report. The route is consistent with several other descriptions.
- Pin elevations look fine (10,033 vs 10,047).

<!-- b008.report.md -->
# b008 report

All 6 routes written. 14 of 14 entries filled, none blank. Every array length matches the route's waypoint count.

- **or_mt_bachelor_trail** (2 of 2): the drive goes to the West Village lot. The summit leg is kept general ("signed summit hiking trail through the ski area") because sources disagree on the trail names and the lift-line routing, and the old Sunrise route is reported as no longer used. Defect: the row has no prose at all (approach, route_breakdown and descent are all null).
- **or_mt_hood_south_side_hogsback** (2 of 2): the leg is taken from the row's own approach and route_breakdown. Defect: there are only two pins for a 3.2-mile, 5,300-ft climb, with no pin at Silcox Hut, Palmer, Crater Rock or the Hogsback. So one card holds the whole climb.
- **or_northwest_ridge** (1 of 1, drive-only): the drive goes to the Green Lakes/Soda Creek TH. Defect: the route has a single waypoint (only the trailhead), no summit pin and no prose.
- **or_south_ridge_route** (2 of 2): the drive goes to the Santiam Pass PCT TH, and the leg follows the PCT north 4.7 mi to the cairned climbers' trail and then the Crawl and the chimney. The pins agree: the summit is due north. I did not state the wilderness-permit rule because I could not confirm whether day use at this trailhead is quota-limited. Defect: there is no route prose.
- **or_three_sisters_traverse** (5 of 5): the legs come from the row's own approach, route_breakdown and descent_text. The pin bearings check out: S Sister to Middle is NNW, Middle to North is NNE, and North to Pole Creek is NE. "Terrible Traverse" is the common name for the exposed snowfield in the breakdown. No conflicts found.
- **or_west_ridge_2** (2 of 2): the drive goes to the Mount Thielsen TH/Sno-Park on OR 138. The leg gives no mileage because sources disagree on the distance to the PCT crossing (the land manager says 2.9 mi, others about 3.8 mi). Defect: there is no route prose. The summit pin's elevation (9,115 ft) differs from the land manager's stated summit of 9,182 ft. I did not adjudicate this, just flagging it.

<!-- b009.report.md -->
# b009 report

## wa_abernathy_peak_north_ridge — 4/4 written
- Drive from the land-manager trailhead page (Twin Lakes Rd → Wolf Creek Rd → gravel FS road). Detailed turn after Wolf Creek Rd kept generic ("follow the signs") because two online sources disagree on the spur road names (FS 5005 vs Left Fork Wolf Creek Rd 1145 / FS 5505).
- DEFECT: `permit` says "No parking pass is needed at the Wolf Creek trailhead"; the USFS trailhead page lists a Northwest Forest Pass / day fee. The same field says no wilderness permit is needed, while the sibling South Ridge row says a free self-issue Lake Chelan-Sawtooth Wilderness permit is required. The two rows contradict each other. I left the wilderness permit out of the drive text.
- Minor: the North Ridge saddle pin has exactly the summit's longitude (-120.5247), and its note says it is an estimate. Gardner Meadows has no coordinate.

## wa_abernathy_peak_south_ridge — 2/2 written
- Drive is about 22 miles (online 21.8; on-file 22-24). FR 44 → FR 4440 confirmed online.
- Note: online the Scatter Lake hike starts on Twisp River Trail #440 (left at the first junction, then right onto Trail #427). On-file calls the start the "Scatter Creek/Scatter Lake trailhead, 50 yards past the Slate Creek sign". I didn't name trail numbers to avoid the mismatch.
- Elevation note: on-file gives about 4.2-4.5 mi to the lake; online gives about 4.6 mi one way (9.2 RT). Not a defect.

## wa_accendo_lunae_lib_west_face_var — 3/3 written
- DEFECT: `approach` says "staying right at the Lake Stuart Trail junction". The actual route is LEFT onto the Colchuck Lake Trail at about 2.25 mi. I wrote "leave the Lake Stuart Trail at its junction" (no left/right), and the prose needs fixing.
- Minor: `approach` says to continue to the lake's "south/east side" for the Aasgard trail. The trail goes around the west shore to the south end. I wrote "around the lake" without a side.
- The TH waypoint has no elevation. `approach_logistics` and `permit` are null.

## wa_agnes_mountain_south_ridge — 2/2 written
- Index 0: High Bridge is reached only by ferry/floatplane plus the 11-mile shuttle (confirmed online).
- Leg 1 compresses the whole multi-day approach and climb into one leg, because the only two pins are trailhead and summit.
- Observation: the route name says "via Asa Peak NE Ridge", but the climbing_route describes the travelled variation over Point 7458 and the south couloir. The name and the described line differ.

## wa_alpine_lookout_round_mountain_trail — 2/2 written
- The drive comes from on-file prose (consistent across fields). The TH waypoint has no elevation (3,900 ft is from route_breakdown).
- DEFECT (minor contradiction): the `approach_variants` baseFinding says the lookout spur "breaks right" AT Ninemile Saddle. `route_breakdown` puts the saddle at mile 4.6 and the Merritt Lake junction at 4.9, BEFORE the final 0.3-mi spur, and `descent_text` has you cross the saddle after leaving the spur. I wrote that the spur comes after crossing the saddle, without placing it exactly.

## wa_argonaut_peak_east_ridge — 3/3 written
- I confirmed the drive online (29 Pines, just under 4 mi, right before the Beverly Creek bridge, 1.4 mi rough spur). The I-90 Exit 85 / SR 970 / Teanaway Road start is standard. I left out the 2025 Labor Mountain Fire closure because it is a dated status claim.
- DEFECT: the "East end of ridge" pin has distMi 6.3, but the on-file approach reaches Ingalls Creek (about 4,200 ft) at about 6 mi, and the basin climb comes after that. 6.3 mi cannot be the 7,700-ft foot of the ridge.
- Possible defect: the "East end of ridge" pin (47.47, -120.858) is only about 0.17 mi east of the summit at 7,700 ft. That fits an 8-pitch ridge base only if the ridge is very steep. Its coordinate looks rounded or estimated. Worth checking whether it marks the ridge foot or the SE spire.
- Naming: the id says `east_ridge` but the name and all prose say Southeast Ridge.
- `itinerary` day 1 says "From the Beverly Creek/Ingalls Creek trailhead system", which blends two separate trailheads.

<!-- b010.report.md -->
# b010 report

## wa_argonaut_peak_northeast_couloir — 2/2 written
- Drive and the TH-to-summit leg written from the row's approach/variant/breakdown plus trip reports (cross Mountaineer Creek, head south, use talus to skirt the slide alder).
- Minor wording conflict: `approach` says follow the trail "toward" the Colchuck junction, the variant says "past" it. I wrote "around the Colchuck Lake junction" so the text doesn't take a side.
- Only two pins (TH and summit), so all the off-trail navigation is packed into one leg. A pin at the couloir base would help.

## wa_beckey_davis — 4/4 written
- **DEFECT:** the note on waypoint 0 (Stuart Lake Trailhead) reads "Alternate access only, used when the standard Stuart Lake Trailhead / Aasgard Pass approach is closed ... via Snow Lakes/Lake Viviane". That text describes the Snow Lakes trailhead, but it sits on the Stuart Lake pin and calls that trailhead the alternate and the standard at the same time. The note belongs to a Snow Lakes alternate, or should be removed.
- **DEFECT:** waypoint 2, "Prusik Peak south face base", has type Campsite and null lat/lng. It isn't a campsite and it has no coordinates.
- Gnome Tarn is 7,415 ft here but 7,372 ft in the bivy data on wa_boving_christensen. Small mismatch.
- The approach prose says "past Isolation/Tranquil Lake". Coming down from Aasgard you reach Tranquil first, so I wrote Tranquil then Isolation.

## wa_big_chiwaukum_west_ridge — 2/2 written
- Drive confirmed (US-2 about 21.5 mi west of Leavenworth, White Pine Road FS 6950 to the end). The leg comes from the row's approach and descent prose. I left out the trailhead elevation because the pin says 2,732 ft and the prose says about 2,800 ft.

## wa_bonanza_peak_north_ridge — 2/2 written
- Index 0 is Holden Village, which has no road. Its entry describes the boat to Lucerne and the bus up FS 8301.
- **DEFECT:** `approach` says to turn "right/south" onto the Holden Lake Trail. Holden Lake and Bonanza lie NORTH of Railroad Creek (summit pin 48.238 vs village 48.20), so "south" is wrong. I wrote "turn right" with no compass direction.
- Route name vs content: the route is called "North Ridge", but the breakdown and descent describe the Mary Green Glacier and a notch on the upper NE ridge, which is the standard SE-side route. Worth checking whether this row is a mislabeled copy of the Mary Green route.
- The trailhead coordinates differ slightly between the pin (48.2016) and approach_logistics (48.1993). That's minor.

## wa_boving_christensen — 3/3 written
- **DEFECT:** the last pin is the Prusik Peak summit, but the row's own baseFinding says the route "finishes on the West Ridge, not the summit" and descends from the ridge. The leg text says it tops out on the ridge; it does not invent a way to the summit.
- The Snow Lakes variant has its own trailhead (47.5441,-120.7095). The pins match Stuart Lake, so the drive goes to Stuart Lake.

## wa_buck_mountain_south_ridge — drive-only, 1/4 written (indices 1-3 left blank as the mode requires)
- Drive confirmed: Coles Corner, SR-207 4.2 mi, Chiwawa Loop Road 1.3 mi, FS 62 about 22 mi, keep left at the final junction.
- **Status note (not put in the text):** as of 2026-07-30 the Buck Creek trail is listed as closed until further notice because of the Little Giant Fire. The row's prose doesn't mention this.
- **DEFECT (pin order and geometry):** the Buck Mountain summit pin (48.0936,-120.9122) is SOUTH of Buck Creek Pass (48.144) and closer to the trailhead. The row's approach goes north to the pass, then back south on the High Pass route into Louis Creek Basin, and none of that is pinned. The summit's distMi of 12 doesn't match the itinerary's 13.5 mi to the basin camp. The footbridge note's "reported gone in 2024" is a dated status claim.
- `approach` calls the road FR-6200 while the variant calls it FR 62. It is FS Road 62.

<!-- b011.report.md -->
# b011 report

## wa_bulls_tooth_standard: 3 of 4 written (0, 1, 2); index 3 blank
- 0: the drive to the Stevens Pass PCT lot. 1: the PCT to the Icicle Creek Trail junction at Josephine Lake. 2: Icicle Creek Trail to Chain Lakes Trail, then over the gap between the basins to Upper Doelle Lake.
- Index 3 (Doelle Lakes to summit) is blank. The route's prose takes the couloir above Upper Chain Lake; it never goes from the Doelle basin.
- **DEFECT, the pin is off the described route:** pin 2 "Doelle Lakes" (Junction, 47.6927,-121.0066) sits on Upper Doelle Lake (about 47.6938,-121.0064), past the pass between the basins. The described route goes from Upper Chain Lake (about 47.6895,-121.0181) up the couloir. The pin route and the prose route are different lines.
- **DEFECT, the row contradicts itself about Bullseye Pass:** `approach` and `itinerary` treat Bullseye Pass (about 6,200 ft) as the couloir's notch on this route. `approach_variants` says the trail pass between the Chain and Doelle basins is a separate feature leading to a different, harder summit. Outside sources put the Chain/Doelle pass at 6,200 ft, which suggests these are the same pass.
- **Minor, distances:** `approach` says "descend the Icicle Creek Trail about 2.6 miles... toward the head of the valley near Josephine Lake". In fact Josephine Lake is at the PCT junction (about 5 mi), and the Chain Lakes junction is just over 8 mi.

## wa_cannon_mountain_northeast_ridge: 2 of 2 written
- **DEFECT, pin 0 is at the wrong trailhead:** it is named "Stuart Lake Trailhead", but 47.53589,-120.8136 is the Eightmile Lake Trailhead (published at 47.5360,-120.8139, and the same as the Cashmere SE row's Eightmile pin). The prose and outside trip reports both start at the Stuart Lake TH lot at the end of Eightmile Road. Because the pin and its name disagree, index 0 describes only the shared drive (Icicle Road, then Eightmile Road FR 7601) and names neither lot. The leg text also leaves out where the route leaves the lot.

## wa_cannon_mountain_south_slopes (drive-only): 1 of 4 written (index 0)
- The drive to Snow Lakes TH matches the pin (47.5441,-120.7097). No defects noticed. `route_breakdown[0]` also offers a Colchuck/Aasgard approach, but nothing in the pins follows it.

## wa_colchuck_balanced_rock_col_east_lake_side_approch (6 wp) — 4 written
- [0]-[3] written. [4] (step -> boulder camp) and [5] (camp -> col) left "" because of an ordering conflict:

<!-- b013.report.md -->
# b013 report

The drive was checked against a trail-association page: Icicle Road 8.4 mi from US 2, then left on FS 7601 for 3.7 mi; Northwest Forest Pass. At the Stuart/Colchuck junction the trail to Colchuck Lake goes LEFT. Past the lake, the trail follows the lake's WEST shore to the boulder field and moraine at the south end.

## wa_colchuck_peak_east_ridge (3 waypoints): 2 written
- Index 0 is blank because it already has text.
- Defect: the approach prose says "Continue along the lake's north shore across a boulder field." The trail arrives at the north end and follows the west shore to the boulder field at the south end. I did not name a shore.
- Defect: approach_variants says "fork right" at the junction for Colchuck Lake, but the trail to Colchuck Lake forks left. I left the turn direction out.
- Note: the index 0 text that is already there contains itinerary and permit material (start times, Colchuck Zone permit) that is not about the drive.

## wa_colchuck_peak_holsten_hilden (3): 3 written
- Defect: approach_variants says "the east-shore path to the inlet". The trail follows the west shore. I did not name a shore.

## wa_colchuck_peak_north_buttress_couloir (3): 2 written
- Index 0 is blank because it already has text.
- Minor: the lake pin says distMi 4.5, while the sibling Colchuck routes say 4.1 or 4.0 for the same lake.

## wa_colchuck_peak_northeast_couloir (4): 3 written
- Index 0 is blank because it already has text.
- Defect: the approach says the Colchuck Lake Trail "forks right (south)" at the junction. It forks left.
- Defect: the approach and the variant both say the east shore is faster ("the west side is brushier and slower"). The maintained trail is on the west shore. I did not name a shore.
- Minor: the base pin is at 6,500 ft, but the prose puts the glacier toe at 6,600-6,800 ft and the couloir entry about a third of the way up the glacier, so the pin looks low.
- Minor: the approach puts the couloir "just east of the Northeast Buttress", but the baseFinding puts the buttress "immediately to your south".
- Minor: the descent calls Colchuck Col "also called Banshee Pass". I could not confirm that name.

## wa_der_dihedral (5): 5 written
- Defect: the approach says the start is "east/right of, and below" Backbone Ridge. Someone facing Dragontail's north/northwest face has east on the left, so this contradicts itself. I wrote only "a couple of ridgelines short of it, lower on the face."
- Inconsistency: the itinerary says the route joins "Backbone Ridge" to finish. The route_breakdown and the descent say it joins the "1972 Northeast Arete". I used the Northeast Arete wording from the breakdown. Someone should confirm whether these are the same line.

## wa_dragontail_peak_backbone_ridge (6): 2 written (indexes 0 and 1); indexes 2-5 left blank
- Major defect: the waypoint list is spliced. In order it reads: Trailhead, Colchuck Lake (landmark), Aasgard Pass, Summit, Colchuck Lake (Water, 5,561 ft), Aasgard Pass (Junction). The lake and Aasgard Pass each appear twice, and the second Aasgard Pass has the same coordinates as the first.
- The ascent never goes to Aasgard Pass; it climbs the moraine to the face. That makes the legs lake → Aasgard → summit wrong for this route. The tail of the list, summit → lake → Aasgard, matches neither the ascent nor the descent.
- I left all four of those legs blank. The list needs re-ordering or de-duplicating before directions can go on it. A likely fix is TH → lake → base of route → summit → Aasgard Pass (descent) → lake.

<!-- b014.report.md -->
# b014 report

The drive and the junction are the facts already verified in earlier batches: Icicle Road 8.4 mi from US 2, then left on FS 7601 for 3.7 mi; the Colchuck Lake Trail goes LEFT at the junction; the lake trail follows the WEST shore. Colchuck Peak (47.4783, -120.8464) and Dragontail (47.4786, -120.8333) were checked online, which places Colchuck Col between them, west of Dragontail's summit, at about 8,070 ft.

## wa_dragontail_peak_east_ridge_aasgard_pass (4 waypoints): 3 written (1-3)
- Index 0 is blank because it already has text.
- Defect: descent_text says climber's-right is "(west)" and climber's-left is "(east)" on Aasgard Pass. The pass runs roughly east-west up from the lake, so the two sides are north and south. The cliff band with the waterfall is on the Dragontail (south) side. The cardinal labels look wrong. I wrote only "stay left", which the ascent variant also says.

## wa_dragontail_peak_r1 Hidden Couloir (8): nothing written, omitted
- Index 0 already has text.
- Ordering fault: the list has two Colchuck Lake pins. #2 is a Campsite at 47.4878 (distMi 4.0, near the south end). #3 is Water at 47.4922 (distMi 4.3, mid-lake and farther north). After that the list goes south again to the moraine at 47.4839. Following the list means walking back north and then south again, so I left every leg blank. Fix: drop the Water pin, or put it before the campsite.
- Defect: the approach, and the note on #4, say to go around the "south/east shore". The trail follows the west shore.
- Minor: the base pin (#5, 6,400 ft) sits at the same elevation as the moraine-crest pin (#4, 6,400 ft).

## wa_dragontail_peak_r2 Gerber-Sink (7): 1 written (index 0, the drive)
- Ordering fault: the same duplicate-lake splice as r1. The Campsite pin (47.4878, 4.0 mi) comes before the Water pin (47.4922, 4.3 mi, farther north), and then the list heads south to the moraine. I left legs 1-6 blank.
- Minor: the start pin (#4) is at 6,700 ft. The other Dragontail north-face starts in this batch sit at 6,400 ft, and r1 and r4 put the face's low point near 6,400 ft. The pin looks about 300 ft high, though a start above the moraine may be what was meant.

## wa_dragontail_peak_r3 Pandora's Box (7): 6 written (1-6)
- Index 0 is blank because it already has text.
- Major pin defect: Colchuck Col (#3) is pinned at 47.4744, -120.8263, 8,500 ft. That is southeast of Dragontail's summit, close to the east-ridge saddle. The real col lies between Colchuck Peak and Dragontail, west of the summit, at about 8,000-8,070 ft (the route's own climbing_route also says about 8,000 ft). The Pandora's Box notch pin (#4, -120.8298) also sits east of the summit. Yet the prose says to traverse northeast from the notch to the summit, which needs the notch to be southwest of the summit. Because the pins and the prose disagree, I left out every cardinal direction on these legs ("toward Dragontail", "below the south side of the ridge" only).
- Defect: #2 "North shore / glacier approach" has a null lat/lng. Its name, and the note on #1 ("head along the north shore toward the Colchuck Glacier"), both say north shore. The approach is via the west and south shore.
- Minor: the descent_text warns about "drifting climbers' right into cliffs near Colchuck Peak" on Aasgard. Colchuck Peak is not next to Aasgard Pass, so this probably means Dragontail.
- Minor: the drive text already on index 0 calls FR-7601 "Eightmile Road". FS 7601 is the road to the Stuart Lake Trailhead (it serves the Eightmile and Stuart trailheads), so this is probably fine, but the other routes in this batch do not call it that.

## wa_dragontail_peak_r4 Triple Couloirs (1, drive-only): 1 written (index 0)
- Defect in approach_variants: it says the trail "crosses to the west side" and then, at the fork, says "Take the left fork, cross back, and continue southeast". The trail to Colchuck Lake goes south-southwest from the junction, not southeast. The variant also puts the lake about 5 miles in, while the other rows say 4-4.5.
- Note: the variant says Icicle Road is plowed only to Bridge Creek Campground at "9.2 miles from Leavenworth". FS 7601 leaves Icicle Road at about 8.4 miles, so the mileages may not fit together. I wrote only a generic note that the road is gated in winter.

## wa_dragontail_peak_serpentine_arete (6): nothing written, omitted
- Index 0 already has text.
- Ordering fault: this is the same splice as Backbone Ridge in b013: TH, lake, Aasgard Pass, summit, lake (Water), Aasgard Pass (Junction), with both Aasgard pins at the same coordinates. Serpentine climbs from the moraine and does not go up Aasgard on the ascent. I left every leg blank.
- Defect: the approach and the variant say "Round the lake's east/south shore" and "Work around the east and south shore". The trail follows the west shore.
- Minor: the itinerary says to "camp on the south shore"; the r4 variant says the campsites are on the western banks.

<!-- b015.report.md -->
# b015 report

Drives checked against land-manager and trail-association pages. Stuart Lake TH: Icicle Creek Rd 8.4 mi, then left on FS 7601 for 3.7 mi; NW Forest Pass; road closed about Nov to May. North Fork Entiat TH: Entiat River Rd (CR 19, which becomes FS 51) 32.3 mi, then right on FS 5606 for 3 mi to the road end; NW Forest Pass. Trailhead elevations are left out of the drive text because the pins disagree with each other (see below).

## wa_e_se_face (legs): nothing written, route omitted
- Indexes 0 to 3 already had text. The only empty entry is index 4, "East/SE Face base", which comes AFTER the summit (index 3). That is an ordering fault: the base is listed after the summit. It stays "" by rule.
- DEFECT: the existing index 2 directions, the `approach` and the `approach_variants` notes all say to skirt Colchuck Lake's "east/south shore". The trail follows the WEST shore.
- DEFECT: the existing index 3 directions say "work uphill to the south and southwest" from Aasgard Pass. The approach and baseFinding say east/southeast, and they treat the west/southwest fork as the wrong one (the col routes). The pins put the tower only about 280 m south-southwest of the pass, so the existing text contradicts the route's own baseFinding.
- DEFECT: the itinerary describes "a roped 5.6 pitch" and says to "rappel or downclimb back to the col and reverse the approach". The grade is 4th and the baseFinding says "fourth class… never go to a col". These are internally inconsistent.
- The Aasgard Pass pin is at 7,841 ft; the prose says ~7,750-7,800 ft.

## wa_emerald_peak_se_ridge (legs): 1 written (index 0, the drive)
- Indexes 1 to 7 already had text.
- DEFECT: index 2 (the Pyramid Mtn #1433 junction) has the note "take the upper-left fork… rather than continuing up the North Fork". The directions say #1437 itself ends at that junction, and index 3's directions say "head west". The note's fork wording does not match.
- DEFECT: the pin elevations do not fit the prose. The prose puts the Pyramid junction "near 6,600 ft"; the pin says 5,300. The prose puts the camp flat at 7,000-7,100; the pin says 6,900, which is close. The trailhead pin is 3,940 ft, but the West Route row puts the same trailhead at 3,700 ft.
- Index 3's directions are titled for "Fox Camp" but describe a clearing a quarter mile west of the junction. The Fox Camp note agrees ("quarter mile past"), so this is fine.

## wa_emerald_peak_west_route (drive-only): 1 written (index 0)
- Only one waypoint. Its trailhead elevation is 3,700 ft, against 3,940 ft for the same trailhead on the SE Ridge row.
- The bivy permit text carries a dated 2026 closure order (Little Giant Fire, Sep 3 to Oct 31, 2026). It will go stale.

## wa_enchantment_enchainment (drive-only): 1 written (index 0)
- The pins are summits only, so all legs stay "" by mode.
- DEFECT: the `approach` sends the party via Stuart Lake, Long Pass and the notch on Stuart's northwest face. Long Pass is south of Stuart, reached from Ingalls, not from Stuart Lake on the north side. That wording looks suspect but was not researched further.

## wa_enchantment_peak_southwest_scramble (drive-only): 1 written (index 0)
- DEFECT: the `approach` says the Stuart Lake Trail goes "north" from the trailhead. It runs south up Mountaineer Creek; the pins put Colchuck Lake south of the trailhead.
- DEFECT: the `approach` and the variant notes both say to skirt Colchuck Lake's "south shore" to reach Aasgard. The trail follows the WEST shore.
- The variant notes say "Stuart Lake Trail #1599 … crossing Mountaineer Creek on footbridges… go left": the creek crossing is on the Colchuck spur, after the junction, not before it. This is minor.
- Colchuck Lake (index 1) has distMi null.

## wa_energizer_bunny (legs): 3 written (all)
- Index 0 is the drive. Index 1 runs from the trailhead via Colchuck Lake (west shore), Aasgard Pass and the Core to Perfection Lake, then up toward Prusik Pass to Gnome Tarn. Index 2 runs from the tarn to the south-face start just left of Solid Gold.
- DEFECT: the approach_variant name and its baseFinding place the route on the WEST face ("left of the corner, i.e. on the west face… faces west… finish on the West Ridge"). The summit note and the first-ascent record call it the SOUTH FACE, just left of Solid Gold, joining Solid Gold near the top. The baseFinding's test ("if you stood in the sun all morning you are on the south face and not here") would send a climber off the correct face. The text I wrote states only what the sources agree on: south face, just left of Solid Gold, under a roof, about 5 pitches, joining Solid Gold's final corner.
- The `approach` calls the Colchuck Lake Trail "#1599A" and puts it "across Mountaineer Creek". The number was not verified and was not used.
- Gnome Tarn is typed "Junction"; it is a camp/tarn.

<!-- b016.report.md -->
# b016 report

## wa_esmeralda_peaks_east_gully (drive-only, 1 waypoint)
- Written: 1/1 (drive to the Esmeralda/Ingalls road-end trailhead on FR 9737).
- Defect: the approach starts at the Esmeralda/Ingalls trailhead, but descent_text loops out via the De Roux Creek Trail "back to the trailhead". That trail ends at the De Roux trailhead about a mile down the road, not at the road end, so the loop does not return to the pinned trailhead.
- The route has only a trailhead pin. It has no gully-base or summit pin.

## wa_esmeralda_peaks_scramble (3 waypoints)
- Written: 3/3 (drive to De Roux, trail to the boulder switchback, scree to the west summit). The pins agree: the boulder is NW of the trailhead and the summit is NE of the boulder.
- Minor: the boulder pin is at 5,450 ft, but the prose says 5,500-5,600 ft. The summit pin is 6,765 ft, but the itinerary says ~6,775 ft.
- Minor: the approach says the trailhead is "about 8 miles up FR-9737". Other descriptions put it 8.5-10 mi beyond the end of pavement. I wrote "about 8.5 miles … about a mile short of the road end".

## wa_fifth_of_july_mountain_scramble (7 waypoints)
- Written: 1 (index 0, the drive). Indexes 1-6 already had text, so I left them "".
- Defects:
  - The Cow Creek Meadows pin is at 5,150 ft, but the approach prose puts the meadows at 6,000-6,300 ft.
  - The approach says to leave the trail "from Cow Creek Meadows". route_breakdown and the pins leave it at Cow Creek Pass.
  - The approach calls Myrtle Lake a 0.4-0.5 mi spur. The pins put the lake on the line of travel.
  - Day 1 of the itinerary claims 1,900 ft of gain to Myrtle Lake at ~3,750 ft from a 3,150 ft trailhead, which is about 600 ft.
  - approach_variants marks the Basalt Ridge variant `primary: true`, but the waypoints describe the Entiat River side.
  - The approach says the trail follows the river's "south bank". Upstream travel toward the NW suggests that is questionable; not verified.

## wa_flora_mountain_southwest_slope (2 waypoints)

- Defects:
  - The trailhead pin has no elevation, and distMi is null on both pins.
  - The permit field says no wilderness permit is needed in the Glacier Peak Wilderness. Other WA rows in this batch say GPW requires a free self-issued permit. Worth checking.
  - There is no pin for Bird Creek Camp or the Enigma saddle, so one leg spans a 2-3 day trip.

## wa_fortress_mountain_east_ridge (2 waypoints)

  - The approach says to stay on the Chiwawa River Trail and go cross-country to a 6,300 ft bench.
