  - route_breakdown says to stay left at the Red Mountain junction near 5 mi and ford the Chiwawa River to a 5,500 ft basin, which is the Chiwawa Basin variant.
- Defects:
  - The variant states that FR-6200 is "closed beyond Atkinson Flat, adding ~7 miles each way". That is a dated claim. Current driving directions run all the way to the Trinity lot with no closure mentioned.
  - The summit is 8,679 ft on the pin but 8,674 ft in route_breakdown.
  - Only trailhead and summit pins exist; there is no col camp pin.

## wa_fortress_mountain_northeast_face (2 waypoints)
- Written: 1 (drive to Trinity, the same text as the East Ridge). I left leg 1 "" because the row has no description of the northeast face itself. The approach only says "same as East Ridge", and route_breakdown describes ridge travel and the East Ridge chimney.
- Defects:
  - route_breakdown and descent_text describe the East Ridge, not a northeast face.
  - The summit pin (48.15917, -120.93389) differs by about 60 m from the East Ridge summit pin (48.159764, -120.933182) for the same peak. The trailhead pin is at 2,772 ft here and 2,800 ft on the East Ridge.

<!-- b017.report.md -->
# b017 report

## wa_fortress_mountain_southwest_face — 2/2 written
- Drive (Coles Corner > SR-207 > Chiwawa Loop Rd > Chiwawa River Rd FR 6200 ~22 mi, keep left at the Phelps Creek fork > Trinity) and leg 1 from the on-file approach.
- Defects: `approach` and the variant's notes/hazards state a dated closure ("since 2026 storm damage has closed FR-6200 beyond Atkinson Flat"). Online, the current closure on FR 6200 and the Phelps Creek road (Aug 2026) is attributed to the Little Giant Fire, not storm damage. The stated cause needs a land-manager check, and the text is a dated status claim either way. Not used in the directions.
- `itinerary` says to camp at "Buck Creek Pass or the alpine basin below Pass No Pass", but the approach goes to Pass No Pass and not to Buck Creek Pass. This is minor.

## wa_fortune_peak_east_slope — 2/2 written
- Drive is the standard Teanaway drive (I-90 Exit 85 > SR-970 > N Fork Teanaway > right fork FR 9737 at 29 Pines). Leg 1 matches the on-file route_breakdown and an independent route description: upper trail ~0.5 mi past Ingalls Pass, then cross-country to the ESE ridge at ~7,050.
- Defect: the summit waypoint has distMi null (the route is roughly 4+ mi).

## wa_fortune_peak_standard_route — 2/2 written
- Leg 1: Esmeralda Basin Trail, then turn right toward Lake Ann, up to the saddle and the ridge boot path north. Online sources agree on the turn to the right.
- Note: online mileage to the Lake Ann turn varies (about 3 mi in one source, 4.8 mi in another), so I gave no mileage. The summit has distMi null.

## wa_full_tilt_tempest_wall_free_version — 3/3 written
- **Defect (approach prose contradicts the trail):** `approach` says "staying right at the Lake Stuart Trail junction". At that junction the Stuart Lake trail goes straight on and the Colchuck Lake Trail breaks off to the LEFT, then crosses Mountaineer Creek again on a second log bridge. So "staying right" sends a party to Stuart Lake. I worded the leg without left or right ("take the Colchuck Lake Trail where the Stuart Lake trail continues"), and the approach prose should be fixed.
- `approach` says to continue "toward the lake's south/east side". The side of the lake the trail follows is not confirmed, so I gave no side, only "along the lakeshore to the boulder field at the south end".
- Waypoint 1 is named "outlet / north dam crossing" and typed Junction. It is really just the arrival at the north end of the lake.
- `approach_logistics` is null.
- `descent_text` hedges ("parties likely have the same option…"). That is speculative wording that renders to a climber.
- Summit waypoint: the route tops out on the summit ridge, and many parties rappel without going to the summit block.

## wa_gorillas_direct — 2/3 written (index 0 already filled; not touched)
- **Defect (prose contradicts itself on aspect):** `approach` files Gorillas in the Mist / Gorillas Direct / King Kong under "the South Face". The approach variant says plainly that the wall is the WEST Face Wall and warns that calling it the south face "sends parties looking on the wrong aspect". The leg follows the variant (West Face Wall).
- Ingalls Pass has distMi 2.5, but the prose says "roughly mile 3" and an independent source gives ~3.3 mi. The summit distMi of 7.5 should be checked too.
- The existing index-0 text is fine but long, and it makes a cross-reference to the North Ridge.

## wa_greenwood_mountain_west_ridge — 1/3 written (drive only)
- **Ordering fault:** the waypoints run Trailhead > Greenwood summit > Dumbell summit. The route passes Dumbell first: up the gully to Dumbell's col, across the ledge on Dumbell's east face, to the notch, then Greenwood's West Ridge. Dumbell is listed after Greenwood as a "landmark", which is not travel order. Following the brief, I left legs 1 and 2 blank.
- Dumbell pin: a rounded coordinate (48.18, -120.8586) with no elevation.
- **Possible defect:** `route_breakdown` calls the ~7,900 ft notch "on the southeast ridge". Dumbell's pin is SW of Greenwood, and the route finishes up Greenwood's WEST ridge, so a notch on the SE ridge contradicts the pins and the route name.
- `approach_logistics` is null, and every waypoint has distMi null.
- Drive: the Phelps Creek road is FR 6211. One online page prints "FR 5211", which looks like a typo. Chiwawa River Road and the Phelps Creek road are currently closed (Little Giant Fire, Aug 2026). That is a dated closure, so it is not in the text.

<!-- b018.report.md -->
# b018 report

## wa_helmet_butte_standard_route (2/2 written)
- 0: drive to Trinity TH (SR 207, Chiwawa Loop Rd, Chiwawa River Rd FR 62, keep left at the final junction). The dated closure claim was left out; it just says to check road status.
- 1: Buck Creek Pass trail to the cutoff, Pass No Pass, then the SW traverse to the block.
- Defects:
  - `approach` and `itinerary` carry dated closure claims ("as of May 20, 2026, FR 6200 closed beyond Atkinson Flats", order number). These are time-sensitive status text in durable prose.
  - The online trail page currently lists the Buck Creek trail as closed for the Little Giant Fire (as of 2026-07-30). This is not reflected in the row, apart from a generic "fire closures" hazard.
  - The road number is written "6200" in the row and "62" on land-manager/trail pages. Both refer to the same road.

## wa_hoodoo_peak_sawtooth_scramble (6/7; index 0 already had text)
- 1–6 written from the row's own prose (the spring, the wilderness boundary, the N Fork ford, the cabin, the boot path to the clearing, then the boulder field to the ridge and north to the summit).
- Defects:
  - **Pin geometry is implausible.** The cabin (7,170 ft, 48.249746,-120.344178) and the "Base of boulder field" (7,700 ft, 48.250472,-120.343958) are about 90 m apart horizontally but 530 ft apart vertically. The summit is about 150 m beyond that, 775 ft higher. Yet distMi says 5.0 → 5.4 → 6.2. The cabin pin is probably too close to the summit.
  - The cabin is placed at "~5 miles" (approach, distMi 5) in some fields and "~4.5 miles" (variant, itinerary) in others.
  - The approach says North Fork Libby Creek is the "first reliable water at ~3 miles", but waypoint 1 is a "reliable spring" at 1.4 mi. The N Fork waypoint is at distMi 2.5, not 3.
  - The approach says the line goes to "the pass on the ridge south of the summit", while the variant says to aim for "the peak's west ridge rising to the north". My leg says only "gain the ridge crest, then follow it north", which both fields agree on.
  - Waypoints 1–3 have null lat/lng.

## wa_iron_peak_teanaway_scramble (1/6; legs 1–5 already had text)
- 0: drive from I-90 Exit 85 (SR 970, Teanaway Rd, North Fork Teanaway Rd, FR 9737). The trail leaves the right side of the road about 0.5 mi past De Roux Campground, per the waypoint's own note. No pass is required.
- Defects:
  - `approach` and `approach_logistics` say the trailhead is "De Roux Creek Campground trailhead at the END of Forest Road 9737". The end of FR 9737 is the Esmeralda Basin TH (47.4366, about 1 mi north). The waypoint note correctly says 0.5 mi past De Roux.
  - Waypoint 4's note reads "Lingering snow lingers".
  - The approach says the creek is "audible for roughly the first half of the hike", but leg 1 and the variant say "first few hundred yards" and "first eighth-mile".

## wa_king_kong_gorillas_direct_direct (2/3; index 0 already had text)
- 1: Ingalls Way #1390 past the Longs Pass turnoff to Ingalls Pass (~3 mi).
- 2: past Ingalls Lake, the climbers' trail to the West Face Wall amphitheater, then the route "to the top of the wall". I deliberately did not claim the summit.
- Defects:
  - **Summit conflict.** Waypoint 2 is "Mount Stuart summit area" at the true summit coordinate, and its note says the route "tops out near the true summit". But route_breakdown's last pitch says "most parties descend the West Ridge gullies to Stuart Pass rather than continuing to the summit". The final waypoint should probably be the top of the West Face Wall, not the summit.
  - Ingalls Pass distMi is 2.5, but the approach says ~3 mi.
  - The index-0 directions text (pre-existing) names other routes ("Gorillas in the Mist and Gorillas Direct parties") and gives no drive. It is not a drive at all.

## wa_leche_la_vaca (3/3)
- 0: Icicle Creek Rd 8.4 mi to FR 7601, then 3.7 mi to the Stuart Lake TH; NWFP/ATB to park; permits.
- 1: the trail to Colchuck Lake. The junction turn direction is deliberately omitted (see defect).
- 2: Aasgard climbers' trail, alder band, base gully, 5 pitches.
- Defects:
  - **`approach` says "right at the Lake Stuart Trail junction". This is wrong:** the Colchuck Lake trail forks LEFT; Stuart Lake is straight. The same error appears in wa_let_it_burn's approach, while that row's own variant correctly says #1599A "splits left".
  - The approach says the log bridge is at 1.5 mi, implying the junction comes right after. The junction is actually at ~2.25 mi.
  - The trailhead waypoint has no elevation.

## wa_let_it_burn (2/3)
- 0: drive (same as Leche).
- 1: the trail to the lake outlet.
- 2: left blank because the approach is spliced. The `approach` field says "cross the outlet and continue toward the lake's south/east side onto the Aasgard climbers' trail ... cut left through brush to the base gully". That splices the two distinct variants: the primary outlet / timbered-hillside route into the west cirque, and the Aasgard-side route that climbs the leftmost gully ~1,000 ft and then contours east. They describe different lines from the lake to the base, so I did not pick one.
- Defects:
  - The splice described above.
  - The same "right at the Lake Stuart Trail junction" error as Leche; the row's variant says left.
  - The descent gives the rappel as "to the east" while its walk-off goes "clockwise". Minor, not adjudicated.

<!-- b019.report.md -->
# b019 report

## wa_lichtenberg_mountain_southeast_ridge — 2 written (idx 1, 2)
- idx 0 already filled. Legs come from the row's own approach/route_breakdown; pins agree (lake due S of the switchback, summit WSW of the lake).
- No defects found.

## wa_luahna_peak_east_slopes — 1 written (idx 0, the drive)
- idx 1-5 already filled. **idx 6 left blank: there is a conflict.** The waypoints describe a Napeequa line (Boulder Pass, then a Napeequa ford at 4,700 ft, then a 7,000-ft "high basin under the east face"). The approach, climbing_route, itinerary and descent all describe a different line: the sheepherder's traverse to the col west of Clark Mountain, then the upper Richardson Glacier to the East Ridge. The approach_variants note admits the waypoints describe "a different line". With no agreed final leg, I wrote nothing for it.
- Defects:
  - The Napeequa ford pin has null lat/lng.
  - The "high basin" pin (48.0562, -120.9712) sits SW of Clark Mountain, near the Richardson/col area. A party coming up from the Napeequa valley floor north of Boulder Pass would have to cross Clark's ridge to get there, so the pins themselves look like two approaches spliced together.
  - The approach prose puts the open basin below Boulder Pass at "~5,000 ft", but the pass pin is at 6,300 ft.
  - The summit note says the Pilz/Butterfly Glaciers are on the north slope. The baseFinding says the record "has already been wrong about it once". Worth checking.
- Drive verified against an online trailhead description: WA 207 for about 10.5 mi from Coles Corner, then White River Road about 10.1 mi to its end, keeping left at the fork past Tall Timbers Ranch, with a NW Forest Pass.

## wa_luahna_peak_southwest_slope_southeast_ridge — 1 written (idx 0, drive-only)
- Same drive as the East Slopes route. No other defects noted beyond the single-waypoint array.

## wa_martin_peak_west_ridge — 2 written (idx 0, 1)
- idx 0 is a boat-plus-bus access, not a drive. I wrote it without a dated status claim, telling the climber to confirm the ferry and bus are running.
- **Status (current, not written into the field):** Holden Village is closed to all guests, including hikers, through the end of 2026. There is no Lucerne-Holden bus. This follows the December 2025 mudslides that caused "catastrophic" failures along FSR 8301, with repairs possibly running into 2027. Rio Tinto has also closed parts of the Railroad Creek Trail and the bypass road between the water treatment plant and the village. The route is effectively unreachable by its stated access right now. Consider a route-level access note.
- Defects:
  - The trailhead pin's name says "Holden Campground", but approach_logistics calls it "Holden Village (Railroad Creek)". The pin (48.1993, -120.7741) sits at or just east of the village; the campground (Ballpark) is up-valley.
  - The pin's lat/lng are stored as strings, not numbers.
  - The array has only trailhead and summit, so one leg covers the whole 7-mile approach plus the climb.
  - The itinerary says "traverse east along the ridge ... to the saddle at the foot of the West Ridge", but the baseFinding says the ridge rises directly out of Holden Pass. This is a minor inconsistency, and I avoided it.

## wa_massie_peak_west_route — 4 written (idx 1, 2, 3, 5)
- idx 0 already filled. **idx 4 (lake → "ridge gain") left blank because of a conflict.**
  - The approach and climbing_route say that from the lake the high route contours **northwest** about 1.5 mi toward Pass No Pass before you turn uphill, then follow the crest **east** for a mile or more.
  - The ridge-gain pin is about 0.5 mi **east-southeast** of the lake, and its note says the high route traverses **east** toward Buck Pass.
  - An online source has the route retracing 0.2 mi from the lake, going SW to a 6,420-ft shoulder, then NW toward Pass No Pass.
- idx 5 is written without any compass direction. The summit pin is NNE of the ridge-gain pin and only about 0.45 mi away, which contradicts "west ridge ... east for a bit over a mile".
- Defects:
  - approach_logistics peakLat/Lng (48.1224, -120.8823) is about 2.6 km from the summit pin (48.1363, -120.9123). One of them is wrong, and I could not find a published coordinate to settle it.
  - The junction pin's distMi is 2.8, but every prose field says "roughly 4 miles" (so does an online source). I omitted the mileage.
  - The junction and ford pins have null coordinates.
  - The trailhead note says the Phelps Creek TH is "~2 mi up Chiwawa River Road", which is garbled: Phelps Creek is near the road end, not 2 mi up it. It also says "named in the beta", which is a pipeline voice.

## wa_mcclellan_peak_southwest_scramble — 4 written (idx 1-4)
- idx 0 already filled. idx 4 carries no compass bearing. The prose says "head SSE" from the Leprechaun/Sprite flats, but the summit lies SSW of the stored "base" pin.
- Defects:
  - The "Base of southwest slopes" pin (47.476, -120.782, 6,900 ft) sits **NNE** of the summit, about 0.25 mi SE of Leprechaun Lake (47.4784, -120.7869). That is not on any southwest slope, so the name or the pin is wrong.
  - Nada Lake pin distMi is 6.5, but the prose says "around 4 miles" (the real figure is about 5.5). Mileage was omitted.
  - Internal contradiction on the finish:
    - The baseFinding says the southwest side (slab plus chimney) means you "stopped short", and to go round to the SE side.
    - The itinerary says to favour the S/SE slopes over the "steeper southwest slabs".
    - The descent_text calls the drop-70-ft-and-wrap-south line "the southwest scramble".
    - The route name is "Southwest Scramble".
    - I wrote only the agreed part: wrap onto the south side, then Class 3.

<!-- b020.report.md -->
# b020 report

Ground elevations below are from USGS EPQS at the pin coordinates.

## wa_milk_n_honey: 3 of 3 written
- Drive: WTA's Colchuck Lake page confirms Icicle Creek Road 8.4 mi, left on FR 7601, 3.7 mi, seasonal closure Nov-May.
- **DEFECT (approach prose):** the prose says "staying right at the Lake Stuart Trail junction". WTA says the Colchuck Lake Trail breaks off to the **left** at about 2.25 mi, where the Stuart Lake trail continues. The leg names the trail to take and leaves out the turn direction. The prose's "staying right" sends climbers toward Stuart Lake.
- The prose's "head toward the lake's south/east side" is vague about which shore the trail follows. The leg says "around the lake to the foot of Aasgard Pass" and does not name a shore.
- The pins match the ground: outlet 5,561 vs 5,560, summit 8,240 vs 8,228.

## wa_mount_berge_southwest_route: 3 of 8 written (legs 1-3); index 0 was already filled
- Legs 4-7 (pass → Berge Ridge notch → saddle → gully → summit) are left blank. **The waypoint chain does not match the route's own prose.** The approach, variant and itinerary all say: drop from Little Giant Pass into the Napeequa valley (about 2,000 ft lost), walk upstream, then climb a boot track to a treeline camp in the SW basin. The pins skip the valley and go straight from the 6,463-ft pass to a "7,840-ft notch" 1.6 mi away. The notch note describes a Louis Creek / Buck Creek / High Pass link, which belongs to a different approach.
- **DEFECT (pin elevations vs the ground):**
  - Notch: claims 7,840, ground 7,474.
  - "Saddle south of summit": claims 7,650, ground **6,643**.
  - "Exposed gully": claims 7,800, ground **6,664**.
  - The saddle and gully pins are about 1,000 ft below their stated heights, so they are misplaced.
  - The summit and pass pins agree with the ground: 7,933 and 6,450.
- **DEFECT:** the summit's distMi is 16.92, against 7.5 for the waypoint before it. That is probably a round-trip figure in a one-way field.
- **DEFECT:** the summit pin's stated elevation is 7,951, but the itinerary gives the true summit as 7,948.

## wa_mount_bigelow_scramble: 7 of 8 written (legs 1-7); index 0 was already filled
- All legs are taken from the on-file variant, baseFinding and itinerary.
- **Minor defect:** route_breakdown gives the Lower Eagle Lake overlook as "~5.5 mi, 7,110 ft", the same height as Upper Eagle Lake. That is likely wrong.
- **Contradiction:** route_breakdown step 4 says to stay "near the ridge crest on the right side of the cliffs". The variant hazards warn that going too far climber's right cliffs you out. The leg follows the baseFinding: right through the larches, then left under the upper band.
- **Contradiction:** the step waypoint note says "~8-ft step **down** into a sandy gully". The baseFinding says the step "is climbed upward". The leg says "climb the chimney step".
- The junction and lake pins match the ground: 7,046 and 7,091.

## wa_mount_fernow_southeast_face: 7 of 8 written (legs 1-7); index 0 was already filled
- **DEFECT (approach prose):** the prose says to follow Phelps Creek Trail #1511 "**south**". The pins and the real trail run north, up-valley toward Spider Meadow. The leg says "up-valley".
- **Prose conflict about the way out of Leroy Basin:** the approach says north/northeast to the col, while the variant says northwest toward a waterfall. The pin is nearly due north. Leg 4 gives no compass bearing.
- **DEFECT (pin elevations vs the ground):**
  - Notch: claims 7,900, ground **6,991**.
  - Gloomy moraine: claims 6,900, ground 6,561.
  - Talus: claims 8,500, ground **7,695**.
  - Summit: claims 9,249, ground 9,109, so it sits off the true top.
  - By the ground, the notch pin is about 900 ft too low. That makes the stated 1,000-ft drop into Gloomy look like only about 430 ft.
- **Possible aspect problem:** the "talus below the southeast face" pin is due **west** of the summit, about 0.36 mi away, and the whole chain approaches from the west. That does not fit a "Southeast Face" name. The approach prose also says the line "curves west onto" the south/southeast slopes. Legs 6-7 avoid naming a face.
- **Inconsistency:** the prose describes the drop as going into the "Ice/Big Creek drainage", but the variant and waypoint call it the Gloomy Glacier. The legs use Gloomy, which is the waypoint's own name.

## wa_mount_howard_south_slope: 2 of 2 written
- Drive confirmed with WTA's Rock Mountain page: 8.5 mi east of Stevens Pass, just past MP 73, a rutted dirt road on the north side, limited parking. The Nason Ridge Trail junction is at about 3.2 mi; turn right for Rock Lake.
- **DEFECT:** approach_logistics.trailhead is **Merritt Lake TH** at 47.7912, -120.8848, but waypoint 0 is the Rock Mountain TH, which matches the ground at 2,656 ft. The two trailhead records disagree. The drive written matches waypoint 0.
- **Contradiction:** the Rock variant says to descend "roughly 900 ft" from the col to Crescent Lake. Its own Crescent Lake bivy note says the lake is reached by "traversing a loose slope around the intervening ridge and dropping about 300 ft". The leg gives no number.
- The summit pin has no distMi.

## wa_mount_lyall_south_route: 2 of 2 written
- Index 0 is the boat-and-shuttle access, taken from the on-file note and the variant. Leg 1 condenses route_breakdown.
- **Possible defect:** the area path files this under Chiwawa-Entiat, but the approach is from Stehekin via Agnes Creek.
- Summit pin ground is 7,842 vs the stated 7,911, about 70 ft low, which is minor.
- The approach says "35-40 miles round trip" but the itinerary totals 34 mi.

<!-- b021.report.md -->
# b021 report

## wa_mount_mastiff_south_route — 4 written (1-4); idx 0 already had text
- DEFECT (pin vs prose): the "South-slope heather/talus" hazard pin (47.8196, -120.9302) sits NORTH-EAST of the summit pin (47.8178, -120.9360), but the route is the South Route and the itinerary climbs the "southeast ridge". The pin is on the wrong side of the summit, or the itinerary's ridge name is wrong. My legs 3-4 avoid naming a compass side for that reason.
- Minor: TH pin elev 3000 vs prose 3,050 ft; approach_logistics TH coordinate differs slightly from the pin (47.7912,-120.8848 vs 47.7905,-120.885).

## wa_mount_maude_r1 — 3 written (1-3); idx 0 already had text
- DEFECT: col elevation conflicts. The summit waypoint note says the Seven Fingered Jack / Maude col is "~7,700 ft"; the primary approach variant says "about 8,150 ft". I left the number out.
- DEFECT: route_breakdown[0] says "Descending traverse across the (South) Entiat Glacier beneath an icefall ... wide, open crevasses", while the baseFinding and hazards describe a descending traverse from the col on snow, ice and loose Class 3 that joins the face partway up. Those are two different approaches.
- Minor: existing idx 0 says FR 62 "roughly twenty-four miles"; sources give ~22 miles from Chiwawa Loop Road. The leg pins have distMi 3.5 to the crossing, but the approach text says "~4 miles".
- The approach field still leads with the Entiat River / Cottonwood option as an equal alternative, although the TH note says it was replaced as the standard approach.

## wa_mount_maude_r2 — 2 written (0, 1)
- The row has only 2 pins (TH, summit), so idx 1 summarizes the whole approach and climb. No Leroy crossing, basin, Ice Lake bivy or notch pins exist.
- Spelling: the row uses both "Icy Lake" and "Ice Lakes"; I used "Ice Lake".
- Distance: summit distMi is null.

## wa_mount_maude_r3 — 2 written (0, 1)
- Only 2 pins; distMi is null; approach_logistics is null; permit is null (siblings carry the Glacier Peak Wilderness permit).
- Itinerary says the couloir is "~1,500 ft"; baseFinding says "roughly 450 m" (~1,480 ft). These agree.

## wa_mount_mccausland_n_route — 5 written (1-5); idx 0 already had text
- DEFECT (waypoint order): Lake Valhalla (idx 3, distMi 2.8) sits BETWEEN the climbers'-path turnoff (idx 2) and the boot path (idx 4). The baseFinding says the turnoff comes before the gap and before the lake, and the lake marks that you have gone too far. As ordered, a climber walks down to the lake and back. I wrote leg 3 as a side trip and leg 4 as "walk back uphill to the turnoff"; reordering the pins (lake last or removed) would be cleaner.
- DEFECT (marker contradiction): the turnoff note says "A small cairn (no trail sign) marks" the turnoff; the baseFinding says "There is no sign and no cairn; the marker is one conspicuous white stone". I named neither.
- DEFECT (prose conflict): the itinerary says the PCT goes "past the lake", then branches onto the spur. That contradicts the baseFinding and route_breakdown (the turnoff is above and before the lake).
- Elevation: the PCT Junction pin is at 4,400 ft, but the approach gives Union Gap as 4,600-4,688 ft. The turnoff pin is at 4,900 ft vs the approach's "about 5,100 ft" gap. The approach says Smithbrook-to-PCT is 1.0-1.3 mi, but the pin gives distMi 1.5 (which is what other sources say).
- The existing idx 0 drive text carries a dated closure claim (FR 6700 storm-damage order May 2026 to Dec 2027). I left it as is; flagging that it needs re-checking.

## wa_mount_stuart_girth_pillar — 2 written (0, 1)
- Only 2 pins (TH, summit); there is no basin, moraine camp or pillar-base pin.
- DEFECT: the itinerary day 2 says the pillar is "11 pitches, crux finger cracks ~5.9". The grade (V 5.11-), baseFinding ("Three pitches, no more") and descent_text (5.10 / 5.11- / 5.10+) disagree with it.
- DEFECT: route_breakdown[0] says "Rope up for the access ramp off the lower North Ridge onto the upper Ice Cliff Glacier". That contradicts the approach and climbing_route, which climb the Ice Cliff Glacier from the moraine camp at its foot.
- The approach says "roughly 2.5 miles past the Colchuck Lake junction (~4,600')", which is likely a garble: the Colchuck junction itself is ~2.5 mi from the TH. I gave no mileage.
- The approach also says "ford Mountaineer Creek's south fork", while the variant says only "cross Mountaineer Creek". The TH elevation is 3,400 on the pin vs 3,540 in the prose.
- descent_text opens by describing the climb pitches (P1-P3) rather than the descent.

<!-- b022.report.md -->
# b022 report

Ground elevations come from the USGS 3DEP point query (EPQS) at each pin.

## wa_mount_stuart_ice_cliff_glacier: 2 of 4 written (legs 1 and 3)
- Index 0 already had text, so it was left alone.
- Index 2 left blank. **Defect:** the pin "Ice Cliff Glacier base bivy" says 7,000 ft, but the ground at the pin (47.483, -120.895) is about 6,560 ft. That is the foot of the moraine and the toe of the North Ridge, not a camp. Every prose field puts the bivy in the hanging basin at about 5,400 ft. The camp's location is disputed, so I wrote no leg to it.
- Leg 3 starts at the toe of the North Ridge at about 6,600 ft. That matches the ground at the pin.

## wa_mount_stuart_stuart_glacier_couloir: 5 of 7 written (legs 1, 2, 4, 5, 6)
- Index 0 already had text.
- Index 3 left blank. **Defect:** the pin "Basin below Sherpa Glacier" (47.4865, -120.8668) says 5,400 ft, but the ground there is about 4,776 ft. It sits on the valley floor, about 1 mile from the off-trail turn. The prose says you follow the valley about 1.5 miles, then climb right into a hanging basin at 5,400 ft. The pin is not in that basin.
- **Defect:** the pin "Off-trail turn" says 5,000 ft, but the ground is about 4,598 ft.
- **Defect:** the note on index 6 says "2-3 short pitches" from the notch to the summit. The approach variant says six pitches of mixed ground, and the route breakdown lists four stages. The note and the prose disagree.
- The Colchuck junction pin is consistent: it says 4,500 ft and the ground is 4,512 ft.

## wa_mount_stuart_the_gendarme: 2 of 2 written
- The drive to the Stuart Lake Trailhead was confirmed: Icicle Creek Road for 8.4 miles, then FR 7601 for 3.7 miles.
- The leg runs from the trailhead to the summit, so it summarizes the whole North Ridge and the choice at the Gendarme. The array has only two pins, so it cannot be split further.
- Minor: `approach_logistics.trailheadDirection` says the trailhead is at about 3,540 ft. Other Stuart rows say 3,400 ft.

## wa_mount_stuart_west_ridge: 2 of 5 written (legs 1 and 4)
- Index 0 already had text. **Defect in that existing text, and in `approach` and `approach_variants`:** they say to stay right at the Longs Pass junction "roughly half a mile in" (or 0.4 mile). In fact the junction 0.3 mile in is Ingalls Way leaving the Esmeralda Basin Trail. The Longs Pass junction is about 1.4 miles farther up Ingalls Way, near 5,440 ft, roughly 1.7 miles from the car. Leg 1 starts from that signed junction and gives no distance from the car, so it does not repeat the error.
- **Defect:** `approach` and `approach_variants` say to head "east/upvalley toward Stuart Pass" once you cross Ingalls Creek. Stuart Pass is at the head of Ingalls Creek, to the west, so east and upvalley point opposite ways here.
- Legs 2 and 3 left blank. **Defect:** the pins disagree with the prose. "Talus Approach to West Ridge" says 6,800 ft, but the ground is about 5,623 ft. It sits east of Longs Pass, near the floor of Ingalls Creek. The prose leaves the path at 6,200 to 6,400 ft, upvalley (west). "West Ridge Crest" says 7,200 ft, but the ground is about 6,282 ft. The array also has no pin for the Ingalls Creek crossing.
- Leg 4 is the climb itself (the gully notch, Long John Tower, the West Ridge Notch traverse, the 5.4/5.6 step). It does not depend on where the pins sit.

## wa_needle_peak_north_ridge: 2 of 2 written
- Index 0 covers the ferry and the shuttle to High Bridge; there is no road.
- Leg 1: about 8.5 to 9 miles on the PCT up Agnes Creek to Swamp Creek Camp, leave the path near 3,200 ft, then up the north ridge.
- Pins agree with the ground: the summit says 7,896 ft and the ground is 7,894 ft.
- Note: the row is filed under Bonanza Peak and named "Northwest Ridge (from Needle Peak)". The approach climbs Needle Peak's North Ridge, and the summit pin is Needle Peak, the first summit of the traverse. That is consistent, but the route's only summit pin is not Bonanza.

## wa_north_gardner_mountain_nw_couloir: 1 of 2 written (index 0)
- The drive was confirmed: SR 20, about 17 miles west of Winthrop, then FR 200 south for 0.8 mile. The pin's ground elevation is about 2,984 ft, against a stated 2,961 ft.
- **Defect:** the trailhead note says the trailhead is "west of Winthrop near milepost 175-176". It is about 17 miles west of Winthrop, past Mazama. I did not confirm the milepost.
- Leg 1 left blank. The prose is vague and partly self-contradictory: it says to leave the Cedar Creek Trail "to bushwhack toward Cedar Creek", then follow the "Shelokum Creek drainage". I could not confirm online where to leave the trail or which drainage to follow.
- The summit pin's `distMi` of 13.1 is far longer than the roughly 4.5-mile straight line from the trailhead. It may be a round-trip figure.

<!-- b023.report.md -->
# b023 report

## wa_north_gardner_mountain_southwest (2/2 written)
- Index 0 drive (Winthrop → Twin Lakes Rd → Wolf Creek Rd → Wildflower Rd/FS 5005 road end). Pass left out: WTA says no pass is needed, and the row's permit line says "some trailheads require a pass"; neither is specific.
- Index 1: Wolf Creek Trail #527 to Gardner Meadows, then the off-trail line west of the stream to Point 8,487 and the summit. The pins agree (the summit is west-northwest of the trailhead).
- Minor defects: the trailhead note says "Southeast Slope-South Ridge" but the route is named "Southwest Slopes / South Ridge". The trailhead pin is at 2,923 ft and the prose says ~2,950 ft (trivial).

## wa_north_star_mountain_cloudy_peak_traverse (2/2 written)
- Index 0 is a Junction (Cloudy Pass), so I described how to reach it: Trail #1256 from Lower Lyman Lake, and Lyman Lake via Railroad Creek/Holden or Phelps Creek/Spider Gap. I left out the closure-order details because they are dated.
- Index 1: up the ridge northeast to Cloudy Peak, off its east side (by the east ridge or south around a cliff band) to the connecting talus, then the final 100 ft of rock. Cloudy Peak's real coordinate (48.212, -120.912) sits between the two pins, so the traverse matches them.
- Defect: the `approach` field is mostly dated closure-order numbers (2026–2027 orders) and contains almost no route description. `grade` is null.

## wa_northeast_buttress_4 (1/3 written: drive only)
- **ORDERING FAULT:** the waypoints run Trailhead, Colchuck Peak summit (i=1), Colchuck Lake (i=2). The lake is listed after the summit, so legs 1–2 are left blank. The summit's distMi is 0, the lake's is 4.1.
- **Defect:** `approach_variants[0].notes` says "right onto the Colchuck Lake Trail" at the fork. The Colchuck Lake Trail is the LEFT branch (the right branch goes to Stuart Lake), as the sibling routes' notes and the land-manager and trail descriptions all say.

## wa_northwest_arete (8/8 written)
- All legs written from the route's own prose (approach_variants, baseFinding, route_breakdown) and the waypoint notes.
- Defects:
  - The fork pin (47.503532, -120.845759, 4,400 ft, distMi 2.2) differs from the same fork on wa_nw_ridge_2 (47.50514, -120.84042, 4,520 ft, distMi 2.3) by about 450 m. Two routes place one named junction in two spots.
  - The basin pin is at 6,800 ft. The prose says the north basin is about 6,700 ft, and the itinerary says the camp is "near water around 6,000 ft".
  - The baseFinding says the snow finger runs up to the SOUTHWEST from the basin. The pins go basin → slab → couloir → notch almost due SOUTH, with the slab slightly east. I wrote these legs without a compass bearing.
  - The "Snow-covered granite slab" hazard pin is not mentioned in any prose field; only its own note describes it.

## wa_nw_ridge_2 (4/4 written)
- **Conflict:** the fork waypoint's note says the Colchuck Lake Trail "breaks left", but `approach` and `approach_variants` say "staying right at the Lake Stuart Trail junction". Left is correct for Colchuck. Because the row disagrees with itself, I did not write a turn direction in leg 2 ("take the Colchuck Lake Trail across the creek"). The approach prose needs fixing.
- Minor defect: `approach` calls the Colchuck Lake Trail "#1599". #1599 is the Stuart Lake Trail; Colchuck is a spur of it.
- The route_breakdown carries pipeline text: "No source publishes pitch lengths for this route, so lengthM is left unset consistently across all sections." That is meta-commentary rendered to climbers, and it names sourcing.

## wa_pinnacle_mountain_entiat_scramble (1/7 written)
- Indices 1–6 already had text, so they were not overwritten. I wrote index 0: Entiat River Rd (County 19 / FS 51) about 32 mi, then FS 5606 about 3 mi to the road end. I did not give the turn direction onto 5606.
- **Defect:** the `itinerary` (both days) describes the OTHER approach: the Entiat River Trailhead, the sheep driveway, Aurora Creek and Pinnacle Creek from the south, with camps on Borealis Ridge. The pins, `approach_logistics` and the primary approach_variant use the North Fork Entiat Trailhead and Saska Pass from the north. The Planner itinerary does not match the waypoints.

# b024 report

## wa_prusik_peak_der_sportsman (3 waypoints): 3 written
- 0: drive (Icicle Creek Rd, FS 7601, 3.7 mi to the Stuart Lake TH, plus pass and permit info).
- 1: the approach over Aasgard to Gnome Tarn / the south face. 2: the climb.
- Defect: wp1 "Prusik Peak south face base" is typed **Campsite** and has null lat/lng/distMi. It is the base of the route, not a camp; the camp is Gnome Tarn.

## wa_prusik_peak_solid_gold (5): 4 written, index 0 already had text
- Defect: the summit pin is the last waypoint, but the route tops out on the West Ridge **below** the true summit. The text for leg 4 says so.
- Defect: the "Base of south face" pin (47.487, -120.7848) sits almost directly under the summit, yet the route starts at the far **left (west) end** of the face, at the outside corner. The pin is probably too far east.
- Minor: approach_variants gives the trailhead as about 3,600 ft; everything else says 3,400 ft.

## wa_prusik_peak_south_face_burgner_stanley (9): 1 written (drive only); legs 1–8 left blank because of an ordering fault
- **Ordering fault (spliced approaches):** the order runs Aasgard Pass → **Lake Viviane** (47.4825, -120.7838, 6,788 ft, distMi 8) → Gnome Tarn (7,415 ft, west of Viviane and uphill) → base. The Aasgard approach on file goes through the Core (Talisman → Rune → Gnome Tarn) and never passes Viviane. Viviane is the lowest, easternmost Core lake, on the **Snow Lakes** approach.
- The Viviane note is also wrong. It says "First lake in the Enchantment core basin after dropping off the pass". Coming off Aasgard, the first lakes are Tranquil and Isolation; Viviane is the last.
- Other: wp6 (chockstone) and wp8 (rappel descent) have null coordinates. The chockstone note says P5, but route_breakdown puts the tunnel behind the chockstone on P4 and the squeeze on P5. Colchuck Lake distMi is 4.75 against 4.1 in the prose. Aasgard distMi is 6.5 here but 5.5 on the sibling routes.

## wa_prusik_peak_west_ridge (5): 5 written
- Leg 3 goes only as far as "to Prusik Pass, where the West Ridge begins", because two fields on file disagree. route_breakdown P1 says the first pitch starts "off the highest point of Prusik Pass". baseFinding says to scramble from the pass around a bump, past a balanced dish-shaped rock and a knob to an overhang, then start. The "West ridge notch" pin (47.4872, -120.786, 7,700 ft) is about 350 m east of and 200 ft above Prusik Pass (47.4873, -120.7906, about 7,497 ft), which fits the scramble version.
- Defect: the approach prose says to traverse/descend "east and south" from Aasgard to Prusik Pass. Prusik Pass is east and **north** of Aasgard (47.480 → 47.487).

## wa_raven_ridge_southeast_ridge_crater_lake (2): 2 written
- Drive confirmed against the land-manager directions (SR 153 → Gold Creek Loop 1029 → Gold Creek Rd 1034 / FS 4340 → FS 4340-300, 4.4–4.6 mi).
- Leg 1: Eagle Lakes Trail #431 to the Crater Creek Trail #416 junction just past the bridge, then to the lake, the SE ridge and the col. I deliberately did not say east or west for the final traverse.
- Defect/inconsistency: this route's primary baseFinding is worded like the Libby side's: a small peak west of the saddle, then traverse east to the true high point. If you come up the southeast ridge from a lake to the SE, it is not clear why the summit would lie to the east of the col. This looks copied from the other approach; it is worth checking against a map.
- Minor: the trail on the ground goes to Lower and Upper Crater Lakes (6,840 and 6,980 ft); the row says one "Crater Lake".

## wa_raven_ridge_standard_route (2): 2 written
- Drive confirmed against the land-manager directions (SR 153 → Libby Creek Rd CR 1045 → FS 43 left → FS 4340 left → 4340-700 right → 4340-750 left to the road end, about 4,430 ft).
- Leg 1: Trail #415 to Libby Lake, the right-hand gully, left onto the rib, then east along the crest to the east summit. An independent ascent of this route ("turned left" at the gully top, which is east when facing the crest) agrees.
- Minor: the approach prose measures SR 153 as "1.4–9.4 miles depending on where you start measuring"; that is meaningless to a climber. `approach_logistics` is null, so the trailhead pin (48.276, -120.29) has nothing on file to agree with; the location is plausible.

# b025 report

## wa_reynolds_peak_scramble — 5 of 7 written (0, 1, 4, 5, 6)
- Blank: 2 (confluence -> "Trail's end / off-trail turn") and 3 (-> upper basin). The on-file fields disagree about where you leave the trail. approach_variants.baseFinding says the confluence (~4,800 ft, i1) IS where you leave the tread. Waypoint i2 says the trail continues to 4 mi / 5,100 ft before it fades. The itinerary puts the leave-point at 5,000-5,300 ft. Online beta agrees with the confluence: at ~2.8 mi / ~4,760 ft, leave the trail, cross the creek and head south up the South Fork. So i2 may be a phantom point, or it may sit on the South Fork rather than on Trail #402.
- DEFECT, bank contradiction: on file it says "stay on climber's right (south) bank". A north-flowing South Fork has no south bank. Online beta says to stay on the EAST side, which is climber's LEFT going upstream (south).
- DEFECT, elevation contradiction: i3 "Upper Reynolds Creek basin" is at 6,700 ft. approach and descent_text put the basin at 5,500-5,700 ft, and baseFinding gives "5,500 to 6,700".
- DEFECT, notch ambiguity: climbing_route puts the gully at a "saddle/notch on the east ridge", then class 2-3 rock. i5 says a Class 3-4 gully splits the summit block to a notch. baseFinding says the north horn is reached first, then the notch, then the south summit. Legs 5 and 6 were written to the common ground (the gully is the crux; the true summit is the south horn).
- Note: i4 is named "Reynolds-Rennie saddle", but the prose only ever says "saddle/notch on the east ridge". Check that these are the same feature.
- Drive: online sources disagree on the road number. One gives FR 4435 / Spur 4435-015 (matches the waypoint note and the land-manager-style directions, so I used it). Another says cross at Mystery Campground on FR-3423. permit field says NW Forest Pass "at developed trailheads"; the i0 note says none is needed here. I left the pass out.

## wa_sherpa_balanced_rock_standard — 3 of 3 written
- Index 0 is not a trailhead, so I wrote it as a short description of how the point is reached, from Esmeralda via Longs Pass, Ingalls Creek and Table Rock.
- DEFECT, misnamed pin: i0 is named "Stuart-Sherpa col". Mount Stuart is WEST of Sherpa, but this pin (47.4739, -120.8865) sits NE of the balanced-rock pins (47.4721, -120.8887). The prose describes a notch reached from Table Rock on the crest EAST of the balanced rock. I wrote to the prose and pin and did not name the col. The i0 note's "west-ridge climbers' path" wording also does not match this east-side approach.
- DEFECT, direction conflict between sources: on-file prose says the notch is "just west of Sherpa's summit", which puts the main summit east of the rock. One online description says to follow the ridge west "past the balanced rock and on to the summit", which puts the summit west of it. I avoided stating which side the main summit is on.
- i1 and i2 share identical coordinates. Fine for a single pitch, but the pedestal pin is not distinct. i0 distMi is 6 with no trailhead waypoint in the array.

# b026 report

## wa_sherpa_glacier (Mount Stuart): 3 of 4 written
- Written: [0] the drive to Stuart Lake TH, [1] TH to the glacier toe, [2] toe to the couloir notch. The text deliberately does not say which ridge the notch is on.
- Blank: [3] top of glacier to summit, because the on-file fields conflict (see below).
- DEFECT: the top of the glacier is described two ways. The wp2 note and the approach_variants baseFinding say the Sherpa Glacier "tops out at a notch on Mount Stuart's upper North Ridge at roughly 7,600 ft". climbing_route says the couloir reaches the Stuart–Sherpa ridge near 8,500–8,600 ft, then you go west past the head of the Cascadian Couloir and the false summit. Online route descriptions agree with climbing_route: the couloir reaches the crest of the EAST ridge and joins the Cascadian route. The North Ridge claim is wrong, and it is a navigation error. The wp2 elevation of 7,600 is also too low.
- DEFECT: the wp1 toe pin is at 6,600 ft, but climbing_route puts the toe near 7,600 ft. The toe pin (47.4785,-120.8842) is also north-east of Sherpa Peak's summit, which is east of where the glacier sits (north of the Stuart–Sherpa col). Worth checking.

- DEFECT: wp2 is named "Stuart basin bivy" at 6,300 ft, but the itinerary puts the East Ridge bivy at about 7,000 ft in the basin below Sherpa's east side. The name is misleading.
- Note: approach_logistics says "via the Ingalls Way Trail" only. That is incomplete, because the route goes over Longs Pass.

That contradicts route_breakdown, descent_text ("west notch") and online sources, which all start the West Ridge from the Stuart–Sherpa col (about 8,100 ft, the notch closest to Sherpa). # b027 report

## wa_southwest_rib_2 (drive-only) — 1 of 4 written

- Defect: the Crystal Creek Junction pin (lng -120.814) sits WEST of both Crystal Lake (-120.8027) and the summit. Crystal Creek drains south from the lake, so where it meets Ingalls Creek should be roughly due south of the lake. The pin looks displaced about 0.6 mi west.

## wa_star_peak_sawtooth_nw_ridge — 2 of 2 written
- [0] Drive is the same as the sibling scramble route's existing index-0 text, so the two routes do not diverge.
- [1] TH to summit via Fish Creek Pass and the NW ridge. Mileage left out on purpose (see below).
- Defects: this row's approach puts Fish Creek Pass at 7.5 mi, but the sibling scramble row puts it at 7 mi and the lake at 7.5 mi. The TH pin elevation is 4,000 here and 3,960 on the sibling, although both pins share the same coordinate. The summit has distMi null. The descent_text begins "no published account gives…", which is pipeline voice on screen.

## wa_star_peak_sawtooth_scramble — 7 of 8 written ([0] already had text)
- [1]–[4] written: the West Fork crossing, the larches, Fish Creek Pass, and Star Lake. Pass→lake is south, which matches the pins.
- [5]–[7] are written WITHOUT compass directions on purpose, because the pins contradict the prose:
  - The prose (and an independent online source) says: climb the bowl SOUTH of the lake to the saddle west of Pt 7912, contour onto the SW ridge, then turn EAST to the summit.
  - The pins say otherwise. The "Notch west of Point 7912" pin (48.25727,-120.41862) sits about 1 mi NE of Star Lake and NORTH of the summit. The "SW ridge talus" pin (48.2540,-120.4236) sits NE of the summit. The pins therefore trace a NE-side line, which is the side whose boulder field the descent text tells climbers NOT to use.
  - In addition, baseFinding says "the peak stands north across the basin", but the summit pin is SE of the lake pin.
  - Recommend: re-place both pins on the SW side, and correct the baseFinding wording.
- [1]: the crossing pin is only about 0.22 mi straight-line from the TH but is labeled distMi 1. That is possible on a winding trail, but suspicious.
- Larch-stand note conflict: the waypoint note says the larches are "roughly two miles below the pass", but the approach says two miles before the LAKE. distMi 5.5 matches the approach. Leg [2] avoids stating either distance.

## wa_switchback_mountain_scramble — 5 of 7 written
- [0] Foggy Dew TH drive: SR 20 → SR 153 → Gold Creek Loop Rd → Gold Creek Rd about 5 mi → FR 4340-200 about 3.6 mi.
- [1] Leg to Foggy Dew Falls written.
- [4]–[6] written: lake → Cooney Pass → SE ridge → summit. The pins agree.
- [2] and [3] left BLANK because waypoint 2 is defective:
  - It is named "Merchants Basin / Cooney Lake Trail #434 split" at 5.5 mi / 7,300 ft, with its pin about 0.9 mi due SOUTH of Cooney Lake.
  - Online sources describe two different things. At about 5 mi the Foggy Dew Trail meets the Martin Creek Trail (right, toward Cooney Lake) and Foggy Dew 417A (left, into Merchants Basin). The standard trail route to the lake is Foggy Dew 4.9 mi + Martin Creek Trail 2.7 mi to the Cooney Lake Trail junction at about 7,240 ft, then 0.2 mi to the lake. The Merchants Basin side reaches only an unmaintained Cooney Lake trail, about 2.5 mi past the 5-mi junction, up near the ridge.
  - So the card conflates the 5-mi Martin Creek/Merchants Basin junction with the 7.6-mi Cooney Lake Trail junction. Its distMi, elevation and name do not describe one real point.
  - approach_variants says the trail "climbs the North Fork… tops out into a wildflower basin… spur branches toward the lake", which mixes the two routes.
- Other mileage inconsistencies:
  - The itinerary says 6.2 mi to Cooney Lake, but the pins say 8.0 mi.
  - Day 2 of the itinerary (12.8 mi) does not add up.
  - The itinerary TH elevation is 3,470, but the pin says 3,400.
- The summit has no distMi.

# b028 report

## wa_the_tempest — 3/3 written
- Drive confirmed (Icicle Rd 8.5 mi, FR 7601 ~4 mi to road end).
- DEFECT (approach + approach_variants prose): says "stay right at the signed junction with the Lake Stuart Trail" — Colchuck forks LEFT. Also calls the Colchuck Lake Trail "#1599"; #1599 is the Stuart Lake Trail, Colchuck is #1599A.
- Minor: summit waypoint has distMi null.

## wa_the_temple_south_ridge — 0 written (index 0 already filled; legs 1-4 left blank)
- ORDERING / SPLICED APPROACH: waypoints run TH -> Prusik Pass (9.5 mi) -> "Core Enchantments / Prusik Pass area" camp (west of and 200 ft below the pass) -> base of south ridge (east again). A camp after the pass that sits on the far side, then back east to the ridge, is not a travel order. Worse, every prose field (approach, approach_variants, itinerary, descent) describes a completely different approach — Snow Lakes Trail to Nada Lake, then the climbers' path up Temple Canyon to Tamarack Meadows — and never mentions Prusik Pass. Two approaches spliced; all legs left "".
- DEFECT in the existing index-0 directions AND approach/approach_logistics: "Follow Icicle Creek Road to its end, about 4.3 miles from US-2". Snow Lakes TH is ~4.2 mi up Icicle Road, NOT at its end (the road continues ~15+ mi; Stuart Lake TH is beyond it at 8.5 mi). The waypoint note says it correctly. Not overwritten.
- Possible inconsistency: route_breakdown (2 items, bolted crux) vs climbing_route (4 items) vs descent_text (2 rappels with chockstone) vs climbing_route n=4 (3 rappels, chains, boulder) — descent counts disagree.

## wa_the_tipping_point — 3/3 written
- Same junction DEFECT in approach_variants: "right at the Lake Stuart junction" (should be left) and "Colchuck Lake Trail #1599" (should be #1599A).
- route_breakdown items 6-8 contain pipeline meta text ("Length apportioned so the pitch total matches the published 1,000 ft route length — the individual figure is not separately sourced") which renders to climbers.
- itinerary names a person as a source ("Abegg logged 17 hours 16 minutes").
- descent_text says the summit walk-off rappel is "optional single ~30 m rappel" — fine; summit distMi null.

## wa_this_my_friend — 7/7 written
- Approach prose correct on junction (#1599 -> #1599A).
- PIN CONCERN: "Route top (East Face, NE Towers)" at 47.4789,-120.83318 is ~0.45 mi WSW of the scree break-off and essentially at Dragontail's main-summit longitude, while the route is a 900-ft face on the NE Towers starting ~300 ft below the top of Aasgard Pass (pass pin at -120.8206). A 900-ft route should not top out that far west; pin likely misplaced toward the summit. Elevation 8,250 is plausible for the NE Towers, not the summit (8,840). Leg 6 text describes the route, not the pin, so it stays true.
- Leg 6 (to Aasgard Pass) written from the descent prose (confirmed online: join the standard summit descent and follow cairned paths to the top of Aasgard Pass).

The pins put the shoulder (48.29876,-120.74427) SOUTH-WEST of the high camp (48.30125,-120.73510), and the summit is also SW of camp. Either the camp pin or the shoulder/summit relationship is wrong; likely the high-camp pin sits too far north/east.

<!-- b029.report.md -->
# b029 report

## wa_upper_north_ridge_w_great_gendarme — 0 written (omitted from JSON)
- Index 0 already filled (not overwritten). It contains meta-commentary aimed at our data, not a climber: "this route is also listed against the Stuart Lake Trailhead on Eightmile Creek Road ... but this upper-ridge variant is the one documented from the Ingalls Lake side". The index-0 `note` is also pipeline voice ("DB currently lists Stuart Lake TH ... appears to be an error").
- Index 1 and 2 left blank: waypoint 1 "Top of Sherpa Glacier notch" (7,600 ft) contradicts the route's own prose. The approach and primary variant reach the North Ridge notch (~8,200 ft) from Goat Pass by crossing the **Stuart** Glacier and climbing a 4th-class gully. The Sherpa Glacier is on the other side of the mountain. The pin (47.47227, -120.89263) sits **south-east** of the summit (47.4751, -120.9031). The North Ridge notch should be **north** of the summit. So the pin, the name, the elevation and the note ("Ascend Sherpa Glacier to this notch") are all wrong for this route. A leg from it to the summit would describe a point that does not exist on the route.
- The second variant (from Mountaineer Creek / the Stuart Lake TH) also lives in the row, and the index-0 note argues about it. The trailhead conflict has been settled only in prose.

## wa_witches_tower_south_face — 6 written (index 0 already filled)
- **Defect:** `approach` and `approach_variants[0].notes` say the Colchuck Lake path goes along the lake's "east/south shore". The established fact is the WEST shore, and the legs use the west shore.
- **Defect:** waypoint 4 is named "Brynhild (Isolation) Lake" and its note calls it the "Largest of the Upper Enchantment lakes". Brynhild and Isolation Lake are different lakes. Brynhild is the small lake just south of Aasgard Pass, and Isolation is larger and farther south-east. The leg says only "Brynhild Lake".
- Minor: the "Base of the tower" pin is east-north-east of the summit pin, not on its south side. The leg's wording ("reach its base", then "swing around ... to its south side" in the final leg) fits the pin and the note.
- Cross-row inconsistency: this row's baseFinding says the south-face line "does not go to the col southwest of the tower". The Southwest Corner row's baseFinding says both lines share the col and converge at the same Class 4 step and 5.5 move. I did not write either claim.
- The itinerary says the trailhead is at 3,540 ft, while everything else says 3,400 ft.

## wa_witches_tower_southwest_corner — 4 of 4 written
- Drive confirmed online: Icicle Creek Rd 8.4 mi, FR 7601 3.7 mi, NW Forest Pass.
- Same east/south-shore error in `approach_variants[0].notes` ("the boot path round the lake's east and south shore").
- `permit` is null, although this route is in the Enchantment Permit Area.
- The Aasgard Pass pin differs from the South Face row's pin by about 140 m (47.480785,-120.822454 vs 47.4803,-120.8206).

- Same drive and legs as the Southwest Corner up to the pass. The final leg follows the row's own col-then-snowfield description.
- `permit` is null (Enchantment Permit Area).

- Leg 1 is blank. The row itself says no approach to the east face is known or described, and I found none.
- Drive confirmed online. The row's prose says "milepost 42" and "fork 0.2 miles up". The online source says near milepost 41 and a junction about 0.3 mi past the tracks, 4.3 mi total. I left out the milepost and the fork distance.

- The drive is the same as for the East Face, and the same milepost 42 vs 41 discrepancy is in `approach` and the variant notes.

<!-- b030.report.md -->
# b030 report

## wa_baring_mountain_oatmeal_man (drive-only, 1 waypoint)
- Written: index 0 (drive to Barclay Lake TH).
- The drive leaves out the milepost and the elevation because the sources disagree on both. The row says MP 42, FR 6024 for 4.3-4.5 mi, and bear left at 0.2 mi. The land-manager/trail-guide description says MP 41, 635th Place NE, keep left at 0.3 mi, then 3.9 mi on FR 6024.
- Defects:
  - The pin elev is 2,200 but the prose says 2,280.
  - The approach and variant date the first ascent to 1961, but route_breakdown says "1960 first-ascent party".
  - route_breakdown names a person as the source of the grade ("reported by Peter Doorish as 5.11c"). That breaks the no-sources rule.
  - A footlog over Barclay Creek has been removed (current trail alert). This could affect the lake approach. I did not put it in the field.

## wa_bears_breast_mountain_infinite_beauty (2 wps) — 2/2 written
## wa_bears_breast_mountain_timeless_treasure (2 wps) — 2/2 written
## wa_bears_breast_mountain_se_mega_slab (2 wps) — 2/2 written
- Index 0 is the Salmon La Sac drive (I-90 Exit 80, SR 903, right turn before the campground entrance). Index 1 is TH → Waptus River Trail 1310 → PCT west to the Waptus River bridge camp → Shovel Creek track → slab → summit block.
- Defects:
  - **Ford contradiction.** The approach_variants for Infinite Beauty and Timeless Treasure say the Waptus River bridge "has been out", so you ford at the horse crossing. The same rows camp "near the Waptus River PCT bridge". The current trail guide puts the washed-out bridge and horse ford on **Spinola Creek**, not the Waptus River. Neither leg mentions a ford.
  - **Rock type.** All three rows call the slab "sandstone" ("one of the longest continuous sandstone slabs in North America"). Bears Breast is granitic, so this is very likely wrong and is shown to climbers.
  - **Distance.** The Infinite Beauty variant says roughly 13 mi to the base, but its approach says 11-12 mi. SE Mega Slab says "south shoulder" and the other two say "southeast shoulder". This is minor.
  - **Summit pin label.** Infinite Beauty and Timeless Treasure name their summit pin "Bears Breast Mountain summit (route GPS reference)". That label is meta-text and is shown to climbers.

## wa_bears_breast_mountain_southwest_face (2 wps) — 1/2 written (index 1 only)
- **Index 0 is left blank because of a trailhead/pin defect.** Since 2007 the Middle Fork Road (FS 56) has been gated to vehicles at the Dingford Creek trailhead (47.51744, -121.45276, 1,400 ft). Dutch Miller Gap Trail 1030 starts there, on the closed road. The row's Dutch Miller Gap Trailhead pin is at 47.5104, -121.3495, at 2,850 ft. That is about 5 mi farther up the closed road, a place a car cannot reach. The row's drive ("~5 more rough miles to the Dutch Miller Gap trailhead (old roadbed terminus, ~2,850 ft)") repeats old directions. The row's own "~15 mi to the gap / 30 mi round trip" only works from the Dingford gate: the gap is at about 14.2 mi + 1 by the USFS trail log. So the pin and the drive disagree with the trail distances. Fix: move the pin to the Dingford gate and rewrite the drive.
- Index 1 is written with no mileage. It covers the closed road, Trail 1030, Hardscrabble Horse Camp, Pedro Camp, Dutch Miller Gap, the left gully, the white fan, the second notch NW of the summit block, and 3 pitches. That leg reads correctly from either start point.
- The route also names a "Williams Creek corridor". The trail follows the Middle Fork; Williams Lake is only a side spur (Trail 1031). This should be checked.

## wa_big_snow_mountain_east_buttress (drive-only, 3 wps) — 1/3 written
- Index 0 is the drive to the Dingford Creek trailhead gate. Indexes 1-2 are blank as drive-only requires.
- I did not state a parking pass. USFS says no pass is required at Dingford, but the WTA trail guide says NWFP/ATB.
- This row's "gated road" prose is correct. It conflicts with the Bears Breast SW Face row, which says you can drive past Dingford.

# b031 report

## wa_big_snow_mountain_east_ridge_hardscrabble_route (drive-only)
- Written: 1 (index 0, drive to Dingford Creek gate). Indices 1-2 blank per drive-only mode.
- Defect: `permit` says "Northwest Forest Pass to park at most trailheads"; the USFS Dingford Creek Trailhead page says no fees are required (WTA lists a NW Forest Pass). The two disagree, so no pass is named in the drive.
- Minor: approach_logistics trailheadLng -121.45437 vs the waypoint/USFS pin -121.45276 (~120 m apart, harmless).

## wa_big_snow_mountain_north_slope_dingford_route
- Written: 4 (legs 1-4). Index 0 already had text, so it was left alone.
- Order Myrtle -> Big Snow Lake -> counterclockwise round Snowflake Lake -> heather bench -> gully -> summit is confirmed by trip reports and matches the pins.
- Defect: route_breakdown[0] says "down 'Snowflake Lane' cleft to Big Snow Lake", which contradicts the approach and the trip reports, where you climb UP a boulder ravine from Myrtle to Big Snow Lake. route_breakdown[2] says you gain the "northeast summit ridge", but the approach says the NW flank. The legs say only the NW flank, which the pins support.
- Same NW Forest Pass / no-fee conflict as above.

## wa_bryant_peak_southeast_slopes
- Written: 2 (legs 1-2). Index 0 already had text.
- Defect (name): the route id says "southeast_slopes" but the name and all the prose describe the Southwest Ridge from Hemlock Pass.
- Possible pin issue: the "Summit block traverse" hazard pin sits about 0.35 mi SW of the summit, yet only about 100 ft below it. That is far from the summit block. Leg 1 is worded to end at the west-shoulder traverse that its note describes.
- Small prose tension: the note says to stay left (south) of the talus field; the approach and climbing_route say to cross the talus or follow the ridge. The leg follows the ridge and the note.

## wa_burnt_boot_peak_north_ridge
- Written: 1 (index 0). Leg 1 left blank: the row has no approach prose, and nothing I found describes reaching the north ridge from the valley. The one trip report I found takes a different line, the pass between Burnt Boot and Pt 5,841.
- DEFECT (important): the trailhead waypoint and approach_logistics name the "Dutch Miller Gap Trailhead at the end of the Middle Fork Snoqualmie Road" as if it can be driven to. The road has been gated at Dingford Creek since 2007 and the USFS now starts the Dutch Miller Gap Trail at Dingford Creek. Index 0 was written as the drive to the Dingford gate, then on foot or by bike along the closed road. No mileage is given, because sources disagree on where the old road end sits (USFS: Hardscrabble Horse Camp at 7.7 mi). The pin (47.5104, -121.3495) matches the WTA coordinate for the old trailhead.
- Missing: elev on the trailhead pin; descent_text, approach and itinerary are all null.

## wa_cathedral_rock_northeast_buttress
- Written: 4 (index 0 drive plus legs 1-3). The drive matches the text already on the sibling 2003-variation row, and WTA confirms the 14.8 mi on SR-903 and the NF-4330 turn. Leg 1 adds the Cle Elum River bridge and the Trail Creek #1332 junction at 2 mi, both from WTA.
- Defect: baseFinding names a source ("Beckey gives the buttress seven pitches") in text that renders, which breaks the no-sources rule. itinerary also mentions "The published trip report's party".
- approach_logistics is null.

## wa_cathedral_rock_northeast_ridge_2003_variation
- Written: 3 (legs 1-3). Index 0 already had text.
- Defect: route_breakdown[0] says "consult a published description for the original approach and start". That is a pointer to a source and is pipeline voice. descent_text also cites "Trip reports on the neighbouring Northeast Buttress".
- Note: this row treats the "original Northeast Ridge" start (snowfield, then exit left into the moat) as the same ground as the Northeast Buttress start. Leg 3 relies on that, since it is the only description on file.

# b032 report

## wa_cathedral_rock_northwest_couloir — 5/5 written

- Defect (minor): distMi puts Peggy's Pond only 0.5 mi past Cathedral Pass; the #1375 spur alone is about a mile plus ~0.2 mi of PCT.
- Defect (minor): this row's Cathedral Pass pin (47.552206,-121.131115) is ~170 m from wa_cathedral_rock_standard's pass pin (47.550692,-121.1307174).

## wa_cathedral_rock_southeast_face_chimney — 3 written (idx 0 already had text, left "")
- Defect: waypoint 1 "Nosh Nosh Wahtum" is typed `Junction`; it is a lake (the NW Couloir row types the same pin `Water`).

## wa_cathedral_rock_standard — 2 written (idx 0 already had text)
- Leg 2 follows approach_variants[0] (PCT < 0.5 mi to the #1375 junction, left climbers' path, spur gully, gully left of South Buttress, SW face), which matches the pins and an independent route description.
- Defect: `approach` says to "angle south/southwest" from the pass; the summit pin is ~500 m north and ~370 m west of the pass pin (NW). I did not state a bearing.

The itinerary also calls the trailhead "Tucquala Meadows" (it is the same place, but under a different name).
