## wa_chair_bryant_traverse — 1 written (drive); leg 1 left ""
- Leg 1 (TH → Chair Peak summit) is blank. The row does not settle which way the traverse runs. The approach says it "begins on Chair Peak's north ridge … then continues along the crest over/past Bryant Peak", which would put Chair's summit early, not at the end. The waypoint note instead routes it "via Source Lake toward Pineapple Pass and Bryant Col", which reads like going Bryant → Chair. Bryant also lies SE of Chair, not off its north ridge. The array has no Bryant Peak or col waypoint, and there is no route_breakdown or descent. Needs a proper route description before any leg can be written.

## wa_chair_peak_northwest_ridge — 3/3 written
- Defect: the "Northwest Ridge base" pin (47.4596,-121.46702) is the peak coordinate copied from approach_logistics.peakLat/peakLng, ~20 m from the summit pin. It is not at the base (the treed saddle on the NW side, reached over the ridge from Thumbtack Rock). Leg 1 is written to the base the card names, so the pin needs moving.
- Minor: the variant says the basin runs "southwest toward Chair's east face". By the pins, Chair is WNW of the trailhead and of Source Lake, so I omitted that bearing.

## wa_chair_peak_voie_de_chaise — 2/2 written
- Leg 1 covers the approach (from approach_variants), the 8 bolted pitches, and the final scramble to the summit. An outside source confirms the route finishes with a short scramble to the summit.
- Possible defect: descent_text and the itinerary say the rappels need two 60 m ropes. An outside route description says most parties rappel the bolted line with a single 60 m rope. The hazard "several rappels leave under 5 ft of rope to spare on a 60m" also fits the single-rope version. Worth checking.
- Trailhead distMi is null (other Chair rows use 0).

<!-- b033.report.md -->
# b033 report

## wa_chair_peak_west_ridge (2 waypoints): 2 written
- 0: the drive to the Alpental lot / Snow Lake TH. 1: TH to summit (Snow Lake Trail, the Source Lake spur, then the NE Buttress start, the slab traverse to the west side, the 220 ft couloir and the crest), all taken from the row's own prose.
- Defects: approach prose is vague about the line onto the west side ("from the basin, traverse slabby ground round"), and I found no outside source for the West Ridge approach, so the leg stays general on that stretch. No pin for the base of the climb.

## wa_chikamin_peak_southeast_slopes (7 waypoints): 1 written (index 0, the drive); indexes 1-6 already had text
- Defects:
  - TH pin elev 2400 ft; the PCT North TH at Snoqualmie Pass is about 3,000 ft (wa_east_ridge_6 gives 3020 for the same coordinates).
  - The "Park Lakes basin" pin (47.4709,-121.3352) sits about 3.7 km WNW of Upper Park Lake as wa_four_brothers pins it (47.4510,-121.2956), and WEST of the Chikamin summit, while Park Lakes lie SE. The "Gully turnoff" pin (47.4751,-121.3280, 5,900 ft) is also west of the summit.
  - Possible ordering fault: the waypoints put Park Lakes (12.5 mi) BEFORE the turnoff "1/3 mi before Chikamin Pass" (13.5 mi). The approach text has the PCT contouring below the Four Brothers before the Park Lakes junction, and the turnoff is near the 5,200 ft pack camp. Northbound, Chikamin Pass may come before Park Lakes. Not verified. The existing leg-4 text ("climb back north out of the basin") depends on that order.
  - Itinerary day 2 says "leave the PCT AT Chikamin Pass", which contradicts the approach and baseFinding ("do NOT continue to Chikamin Pass").
  - Turnoff distance: "about half a mile short" in the approach, "~1/3 mi" in the waypoint. The turnoff pin elev is 5,900, against a "~5,200 ft pack camp" in the approach.
  - route_breakdown describes the Mineral Creek approach and the variant marked primary is Mineral Creek, but the waypoints and approach_logistics follow the PCT from Snoqualmie.
  - The TH note says "$5/day Recreation Pass". The pass in use is the Northwest Forest Pass.

## wa_chimney_rock_east_face_direct (3 waypoints): 1 written (index 0, the drive to Pete Lake TH)
- Left 1 and 2 blank because the pin contradicts the route. The "East Face Direct - base of climb" pin (47.50589,-121.28827) lies about 380 m SSW of the main summit (47.509135,-121.285965) and slightly WEST of it. The route's own prose puts the start on the EAST face, directly beneath the summit, above the Chimney Glacier (east side). A leg written to "the east face beneath the summit" would contradict the pin. The pin is probably on the wrong side or near the South Peak/U-Gap. The row also has no route_breakdown or climbing_route for the Direct line.

## wa_east_face_6 (Chimney Rock East Face, 7 waypoints): 7 written
- Drive, Pete Lake Trail, the PCT junction near Lemah Meadow, north on the PCT to the avalanche-path switchback, the climbers' path to Sunrise Knob, the glacier to the moat, and the moat via the U-Gap, South Peak ledges, Main Notch, Key Ledge and three pitches to the summit.
- Defects:
  - Avalanche-path pin elev 3,800 ft; the variant prose puts the hairpin at about 3,360 ft. Elevation is left out of the text.
  - The approach says the PCT junction is "roughly 2 more miles" past Pete Lake "near Escondido Ridge", but the waypoint distMi gives 0.8 mi (4.5 to 5.3), with the junction at Lemah Meadow. Mileage is left out.
  - The start of the climbers' path is described three ways: the approach says it starts on the true-right side and fords back after 200 yards, the note says it drops to a log crossing, and the variant says a logjam about 20 yards inside the forest. The text omits the crossing detail.
  - Camp: the approach says to climb the moraine to a 5,400 ft boulder high camp, but the variant says to camp below the moraine at 4,650-4,800 and drop 200 ft to the glacier. The text avoids both.
  - PCT junction, Sunrise Knob and moat pins have null coordinates.
  - The descent text says the Direct variant uses 5-6 rappels "straight down the glacier-facing slabs". Fine, but note that it describes another route.

## wa_east_ridge_6 (Mount Thomson East Ridge, 2 waypoints): 2 written
- Drive to PCT North TH; TH to summit via the Katwalk, Ridge/Gravel Lakes, Bumblebee Pass, the south-face basin, the ESE notch and the class 4 step.
- Defects: the approach gives about 4,900 ft of round-trip gain while the variant gives "about 3,600 ft of cumulative gain". Not used in the text.

## wa_four_brothers_southwest_route (8 waypoints): 1 written (index 0, the drive to Mineral Creek TH); 1-7 already had text
- Defects:
  - The waypoint 6 note says to climb "toward the saddle southwest of the upper lake", but the baseFinding says to gain the crest at the saddle NW of the group, between Chikamin and the Four Brothers. Also, the pack-camp pin (47.4621) is NORTH of Upper Park Lake (47.4510), not SW.
  - The existing leg 7 says "keep to the north side of the Four Brothers on the way up" from the Park Lakes side. The basin lies S/SE of Chikamin Ridge, so staying on the north side means crossing the ridge first. Worth a check.
  - The Mineral Creek trail was listed as closed for the Three Queens Fire (30 Jul 2026). This is a dated status and was not put in the text.

<!-- b034.report.md -->
# b034 report

## wa_garfield_mountain_infinite_bliss (2 waypoints): 2 written
- i0 drive and i1 leg written.
- DEFECT: the approach_variants notes say "turn right onto road 5620" at the junction past the Taylor River bridge. Per USFS/WTA, FR 5620 is the Taylor River Road, which is the LEFT fork. The pin (47.54604, -121.52151) sits about 1 mile ESE of the junction on the Middle Fork Road (FR 56, the right fork), which matches an independent report that puts the parking 1.1 mi up the Middle Fork Road. "Right" is correct, but the road number is wrong. The `approach` field's "Follow NF-110/NF-5620 uphill" is also suspect: no road walk is needed beyond 100-200 yards. I wrote "keep right on the Middle Fork Road" and left out the 5620 number.

## wa_garfield_mountain_preiss_route (1 waypoint, drive-only): 1 written
- Drive to the Garfield Ledges trailhead. The pin matches the WTA trailhead coordinate 47.5553, -121.5355 exactly.
- Note: the waypoint name says "NF-110 spur leaves here; park about 1/3 mile up the spur". I could not verify that, so the drive ends at the trailhead lot and does not mention the spur.

## wa_garfield_mountain_southwest_ledges (2 waypoints): 2 written
- Same drive as Infinite Bliss (same pin). The i1 leg follows the IB climbers' path to the point where the route leaves it, just before the path turns left to the base, which matches waypoint 1's name and the route_breakdown.
- The pin for waypoint 1 is the same coordinate as the IB base, but the name says it is the turnoff just before the base. That is a minor co-location and is not a problem.
- The same 5620 road-number issue may exist upstream. This row has no drive prose.

## wa_grotto_mountain_e_route: 0 written (omitted)
- Only i3 (Klinger Lake) was empty. I left it blank.
- DEFECT (elevation/ordering): the Klinger Lake pin is at 4,705 ft. The next waypoint, "Klinger Ridge meadow gain", is at 4,600 ft, and its existing text says it is reached by a "rising traverse" from the tarn "below the ridge". The lake cannot be both below the ridge and 100 ft higher than the point you rise to. The prose never routes a party to Klinger Lake on the ascent; the descent text mentions the tarn only as a place to drop into.
- Also: the existing i0 "directions" text does not describe the drive. It only says "~6.5 miles of gravel... confirm with ranger district". The real turn sequence (US-2 → Beckler River Rd FS-65 → Eagle Creek Rd FS-6514 → spur 410) is only in the note. I did not overwrite it.

## wa_gunnshy_peak_standard_route (3 waypoints): 2 written
- i0 already had text, so I returned "". i1 and i2 written from the row's approach_variants, cross-checked against a trip report (Barclay Creek logs, 3,600 ft headwall, waterfall gully, Tailgunner south ridge, saddle 5,400, Point 5,760 bypassed 200 ft on the east, rising traverse above Gunn Lake to the east ridge at 5,950, west along the crest to the summit).
- Minor inconsistency: route_breakdown describes the summer variant as descending "to the Gunn Lake basin and traverses west", while the baseFinding describes a rising traverse above Gunn Lake. I wrote the "rising traverse above" version, which the trip report supports. The waypoint 1 pin has no elevation.

## wa_guye_peak_improbable_traverse (4 waypoints, drive-only): 1 written
- i0 drive to the Alpental lot / Snow Lake TH, with a warning about the private Strasse roads. That restriction is a long-standing private-road policy and is stated in the row.
- Note: the row's `approach` still says "walk through residential area on Ober Strasse Road", which contradicts its own approach_variants (no walking or driving on Strasse roads since 2021). The waypoint 0 note also says "via the Ober Strasse approach". Both should be corrected.

<!-- b035.report.md -->
# b035 report

## wa_guye_peak_r1 (7 waypoints): 5 written (legs 2-6)
- Index 0 already had text, so it was left alone.
- Leg 1 (trailhead to talus cone) is blank because the record contradicts itself. `approach` describes a steep climbers' path leaving 100 ft south of the Snow Lake sign up Guye's "southeast flank", reaching a junction (right fork to Guye/Cave Ridge, left fork to Commonwealth Basin/PCT). That sounds like the Cave Ridge path. `approach_variants` says there is NO established path to the talus and that the direct line crosses forbidden private roads. Index 0's existing text says the path leaves "50 ft downhill" from the trailhead. So the record gives three different starts.
- Defect: the summit pin's note calls it the "True summit". But `descent_text` says the route tops out on the SOUTH summit and that the north summit may be higher. Waypoint 6's note also calls the north summit the "north/true summit".

## wa_guye_peak_r2 (3 waypoints): 3 written
- Defect: the waypoints at index 1 and 2 have NO lat/lng.
- Defect, a direction that contradicts the pins: the saddle note, `approach`, `approach_variants` and `baseFinding` all say "turn right (north)" at the Cave Ridge saddle toward Guye's north summit. Guye is SOUTH of the Cave Ridge saddle (r1's saddle pin is at 47.4467; the summit is at 47.442). The leg I wrote leaves out the compass word.
- Defect: `approach` calls the boot path "signed", while the variant and the itinerary call it "unmarked"/"unsigned". The leg I wrote avoids saying either.
- Defect: `descent_text` calls the route "North Rib", which does not match its name.

## wa_guye_peak_southeast_gully (2 waypoints): 1 written (the drive)
- Leg 1 is blank. The gully finishes on the ridge between the true and false summits, "not on a summit", and nothing in the record describes the step from there to the summit card.
- Defect: this row's PCT North trailhead pin (47.428404, -121.415483) is about 230 m from the PCT North pin on wa_huckleberry_mountain_east_ridge (47.42733, -121.41286). It is one trailhead with two coordinates.
- Note: `descent_text` says the west descent leaves "a road walk of roughly a mile" back to the PCT lot. That is plausible but I did not check it.

## wa_hibox_mountain_standard (2 waypoints): 2 written
- Defect: `approach` says the boot path climbs "about 500 vertical feet" from the ~3,300 ft clearing to a cliff band "around 5,300 ft". Those figures cannot both be right. I left elevations out of the leg.
- Minor: the trailhead pin (47.4007, -121.2839) and `approach_logistics` (47.4012, -121.2848) are about 80 m apart.
- Minor: the itinerary describes gaining "Box Ridge" and following its crest east. `approach`/`climbing_route` describe a talus traverse east under the summit block instead. I followed `approach`/`climbing_route`.

## wa_hourglass_gully_winter (2 waypoints): 2 written
- Defect: `approach` describes "~5,500 ft gain ... sustained moderate rock climbing higher up". That is summer/Main Peak-style prose, not this winter snow gully. The variant gives 6,000+ ft, the grade field says "4th", and the summit is 5,979 ft (pin) against ~5,991 ft (approach).
- Defect: the itinerary and `descent_text` mention traversing an "East Ridge" after the gully, which `route_breakdown` does not have. The leg I wrote follows `route_breakdown`: exit onto the upper ridge, then the knife-edged crest to the summit.
- Defect: `route_breakdown` says "3,500-4,000 ft gain" from the lake to the saddle, which cannot be right for a lake at about 2,500 ft below a peak under 6,000 ft.

## wa_huckleberry_mountain_east_ridge (2 waypoints): 2 written
- Defect: `approach` gives about 2,700 ft of gain to a camp at Huckleberry Flats, then 1,900 ft more to the base. The variant gives 4,600 ft total. The camp is also named differently in different fields: Huckleberry Flats in `approach`, Ridge/Gravel Lake in the itinerary. I left out gain figures and camp names.
- Note: the `route_breakdown` entry includes pipeline arithmetic: "derived by subtracting the sourced 60 ft crux section from the 350 ft total route length". This is meta-text that renders to the climber.
- Note: `approach_variants` names "the alternative Beckey gully" in its hazards list and says "the older Beckey line" in `baseFinding`, so a person is named as a source on screen.

<!-- b036.report.md -->
# b036 report

## wa_huckleberry_mountain_south_face — 2/2 written
- Drive (PCT North TH, Exit 52) and the single trailhead-to-summit leg (PCT above Joe Lake, heather/scree to the face, 2 pitches 5.7).
- No defects beyond an approximate pitch split already flagged in its own breakdown.

## wa_huckleberry_mountain_west_route — 0 written (omitted; index 0 already filled)
- Legs 1-3 left blank because the array is not coherent:
  - The "Joe Lake" pin (47.46778,-121.3375, 4,629 ft) sits at the lake itself. The PCT does not reach the lake: it runs well above it, and the route leaves the PCT at a meadow northeast of the lake. Leg 2 ("West Face Talus Basin") therefore starts from the trail, not from the lake pin.
  - The Joe Lake note says the "PCT ... drops to the west end of Joe Lake". That is wrong and contradicts the row's own approach ("where the trail runs above Joe Lake", "water ... at Joe Lake below the trail").
  - The "West Face Talus Basin" pin has null lat/lng.
  - Leg 3 has conflicting descriptions. The grade is "Class 4" and the itinerary describes class 3-4 scrambling, a class 4-5 summit block and a 60 m rappel into a notch. The route_breakdown and summit note describe a 4-pitch, ~450 ft roped West Face (first climbed 2001, loose 5th-class dihedrals). The itinerary mentions "Huckleberry Flats", which belongs to the East Ridge approach (Kendall Pass/Huckleberry Flats), so it looks spliced from another route.
  - The Joe Lake distMi of 9 conflicts with the approach's "about 10 miles".

## wa_iron_cap_mountain_south_route — 4/4 written
- Drive: confirmed online (US 2 1.9 mi east of Skykomish; FR 68; right at the Y at 3.5 mi; left on FR 6835 at 4.7 mi before the bridge; 1.9 mi to the TH).
- Defect (minor): the route name is "West Ridge / North Ridge via Chetwoot Lake", but the id is `..._south_route`.
- Defect (minor): the Big Heart Lake → Chetwoot pins run SSE, which matches the prose. The Chetwoot note says camps sit "just south of Little Chetwoot Lake", which is consistent.

## wa_j_tnar — 3/6 written (drive, trailhead→Lake Serene, lake→base cirque)
- Legs 3-5 left blank:
  - The "Jötunheim Wall Topout (Middle Peak)" is listed at 5,500 ft but has null coords. The next card, the "Middle Peak (Mount Index)" summit, is listed at 5,406 ft, so the topout is placed 94 ft ABOVE the summit it tops out onto. One of the two elevations is wrong.
  - It is unclear what the "North Norwegian Buttress" Feature pin (4,801 ft) marks: a point partway up the wall, or the buttress top. Its position relative to the base pin, which lies south of it, does not make the leg obvious.
- Approach prose conflict (minor): the main `approach` sends parties "toward its south/southeast end", while the approach variant and the trailhead note say to go around the west (right-hand) side. The base pin lies SW of the lake pin, so I wrote the west-side path, which agrees with the variant, the note and the pins.

## wa_kendall_peak_cliff_north_face — 1/2 written (drive only)
- Leg 1 left blank. The "Kendall Cliff (North Face base)" pin (47.44291,-121.38483) is about 20 m from the Kendall Peak SUMMIT coordinate (47.443025,-121.384905). The prose puts the cliff base in a clearing on the old Commonwealth Creek trail, short of the creek, which is low in Commonwealth Basin far below the summit. The pin contradicts the prose.
- The trailhead and base pins have no elevation, and the grade is null.
- The approach prose says the old Commonwealth Creek trail leaves the PCT at "an early fork". The Kendall Standard row puts the Commonwealth Basin junction at 2.6 mi. This is possibly a different (old, abandoned) trail, but it is unconfirmed.

## wa_kendall_peak_standard — 1/3 written (drive-only mode)
- Ordering fault confirmed. The "Kendall Katwalk (PCT)" pin (47.4518, 5,387 ft) comes before the summit, but the standard scramble leaves the PCT at the stump at ~5,200 ft, BEFORE the Katwalk. The row's own baseFinding says "If you have crossed the Kendall Katwalk you have gone a long way past the turn."
- The itinerary says the trailhead is "the Commonwealth Basin/Alpine Lakes trailhead". It is the PCT North trailhead. Cosmetic.

Grades disagree: Class 2-3 in grade, "3rd/4th-class steps" in route_breakdown, "light 3rd/easy 4th" in itinerary. Itinerary day 1 gives 3,200 ft gain to Jade Lake, but the approach and pin elevations give about 3,000 ft (and the note says 2,500). The Jade Lake note says "steep 2,400 ft climb in 3 miles" while the pins put the river crossing at 2,250 and Jade at 4,650, so 2,400 is fine; just a minor check.

## wa_lemah_mountain_east_route (8 wp) — 3 written
- Written: [0] drive to the Pete Lake TH (Exit 80 / SR 903 / FR 46 / FR 4616), [6] moat -> cliff bands -> the Lemah Two/Main col, [7] col -> summit along the NW ridge. Leg 6 uses no compass direction because the sources disagree (see below).
- Blank: [1]-[5] already had text.
- Defects:
  - PCT direction CONFLICT. This row's approach says "Turn north (right) on the PCT". The sibling row wa_lemah_two_goatshead_spire says "Turn left (south) on the PCT ... to Lemah Creek". This row's own variant says "it is unclear whether the Lemah junction is north or south". The existing text in leg [2] says "staying left at each trail fork", while the Lemah Two row says "stay right without crossing the creek". Someone needs to settle which way it goes.
  - The traverse to the col is "southwest" in approach and route_breakdown, but the variant says you "traversed north under Lemah Two". The Lemah 1-5 numbering is never made consistent.

  - Pins [2]-[6] all have null lat/lng.

## wa_little_big_chief_mountain_northeast_face (2 wp) — 2 written
- [0] drive (Exit 34 / 468th Ave / FS 56 about 12 mi, then about 6 mi of potholed gravel to the gated parking). [1] trail plus scramble to the summit. The summit pin is SW of Dutch Miller Gap (about 47.5356, -121.2436), which agrees with "immediately southwest of the gap".
- Defects:
  - The trailhead naming is muddled. The approach says "From the Dutch Miller Gap Trailhead", while the waypoint is named "Dingford Creek / Middle Fork Snoqualmie Trailhead". The note cites "Middle Fork Trail 1003", while the approach uses Trail 1030. The pin at the gated parking near Dingford Creek is consistent with today's start, about 14.5 mi one way.
  - The permit field says "No recreation pass is required at the Dingford Creek Trailhead", but the land-manager and hiking listings require a NW Forest Pass / America the Beautiful pass. I left the pass out of the drive text.

## wa_mount_daniel_daniel_glacier (3 wp) — 2 written
- Blank: [0] already had text.

The trail to Cathedral Pass is the Cathedral Rock Trail #1345; the Deception Pass trail is a different trail from the same trailhead, and a climber following the row's naming could take the wrong trail.

  - The itinerary gives Cathedral Pass as ~5,550 ft, but the approach gives ~5,500 ft (other listings say 5,600).

<!-- b039.report.md -->
# b039 report

## wa_mount_price_hester_lake_route — 1 of 2 written (drive only)
- Leg 1 (TH -> summit) is left blank because the on-file fields disagree about the route. `approach`, `itinerary`, `descent_text` and route_breakdown[1] describe the Hester Lake line: Dingford Creek Trail, then about 3 mi to the Hester Lake Trail junction, then Little Hester Lake, the boulder field south of it to the east ridge, the 5,400 ft col and the summit. **route_breakdown[0] ("Price Creek gully, ~3,200-3,800 ft — the most technical section") belongs to a different route.** It is the Price Lake / Price Creek scramble up from the Middle Fork, not the Hester Lake route. Remove it, and this text can then be used safely: "Hike the Dingford Creek Trail about 3 miles to the Hester Lake Trail junction and follow that boggy path across two forks of Dingford Creek to Little Hester Lake. Climb the boulder field south of the lake to the east ridge east of the 5,440 ft point, traverse the steep north side to the 5,400 ft col just east of the summit, and scramble the final ridge to the top."
- Summit pin distMi is null.
- The drive is confirmed by the land manager's page: Exit 34, 468th Ave SE, about 0.6 mi, then FR 56 for about 12 mi to the Taylor River junction, then about 5-6 mi to Dingford, with parking on the right. Sources disagree on the road number past that junction (FR 56 or 5620), so the drive text does not give one.

## wa_mount_roosevelt_standard — 8 of 8 written
- **Snow Lake elevation is wrong in `approach`.** It says "Snow Lake at 3,832 ft", but the lake is about 4,000 ft, and the pin's 4,020 is correct.
- **Snow Lake distance:** the pin's distMi is 2.6, and `approach` also says 2.6 mi. The trail reaches the lake at about 2.9 mi (1.75 mi plus about 0.67 mi plus about 0.5 mi) and the outlet at about 3.4 mi, so the itinerary's "2.6 mi" is likely short.
- **`approach` calls the Snow Lake to Gem Lake section a "climbers' path".** It is maintained trail (#1013 continues to Gem Lake, via a signed junction and a log bridge). The Snow Lake waypoint note also says to take "the boot path along the north shore ... rather than the main loop trail around the outlet". That reads as wrong, because the maintained trail is the way to Gem Lake. Leg 2 is worded only with what everyone agrees on: around the north end, then about a mile through a rock field and meadows.
- Pins 3 to 7 sit on one nearly straight east-to-west line at about lat 47.4692-47.4697. They look interpolated or synthetic rather than measured. They do not contradict the direction of travel (west from Gem Lake to the summit).
- route_breakdown calls the gully "class 2", while the hazard note and the route grade say Class 3. The leg text avoids stating a class for the gully.

## wa_mount_teneriffe_kamikaze_trail — 1 of 4 written (drive only)
- **Ordering fault, so every leg is blank.** Waypoint 1 "Teneriffe Falls (Kamikaze Falls)" (distMi 2.4) comes before waypoint 2 "Teneriffe Falls / Kamikaze Trail junction" (distMi 2.8), so the falls are listed before the junction that leads to them. Both pins also have **identical coordinates and elevation** (47.50209, -121.70807, 2,633 ft).
- **Trailhead location error in prose.** `approach_variants` says the Teneriffe lot is "three miles past the neighbouring peak's trailhead". It is 2.9 mi up SE Mount Si Road from North Bend Way, and you pass the Little Si and Mount Si trailheads on the way. It is not 3 mi beyond the Mount Si trailhead.

## wa_mount_teneriffe_standard_route — 1 of 4 written (drive only)
- **Ordering fault, so every leg is blank.** Junction (2.7) and falls (2.8) share **identical coordinates** (the falls themselves, at 2,633 ft). The standard summit trail does not pass the falls: the falls trail is a side branch off the old road grade at about 1.5-2 mi. So "falls" then "summit" is not a leg that any party walks.
- **Trailhead prose is wrong in the same way.** `approach` and `approach_logistics.trailheadDirection` say "continue about 2.9 miles past the Mount Si trailhead". The correct reading is 2.9 mi along SE Mount Si Road, passing the Little Si and Mount Si trailheads.
- `approach` mis-sequences the trail: the switchbacks start after the Roaring Creek Trail intersection, and the summit is reached by going right at a T-junction at about 4.7 mi. The real trail is described as access path 0.5 mi, then road grade about 1.5 mi, then 2.7 mi of switchbacks, then 2.3 mi to the summit, for 6.5 mi in all.

## wa_mount_thomson_west_ridge — 0 written (omitted)
- Index 0 already had text.
- **Ordering fault, so all legs are blank.** Gravel Lake (wp 2, distMi 7.4, lat 47.46498) is listed before Ridge Lake (wp 3, distMi 7.6, lat 47.46136). Northbound on the PCT, the trail reaches Ridge Lake first, and the pins agree: Ridge Lake is south of Gravel Lake.
- The Bumblebee Pass pin has null lat and lng.
- The Kendall Katwalk is described inconsistently: the waypoint note says "Kendall Peak's north ridge", while the approach says "Kendall Peak's south side".

## wa_new_york_gully — 2 of 2 written
- **The trailhead note contradicts the approach.** The note says the standard approach is "via Snow Lake trail then Phantom Slide/Source Lake basin". The approach goes straight up the hillside from the Alpental lots by the waterfalls and the Phantom Slide, and does not use the Snow Lake trail.
- The summit waypoint has distMi 0.
- The summit is not the top of the technical route. The climb ends at a col between gendarmes, and the summit is reached by rappelling into Enigma Gully and climbing back up its snow, as `descent_text` describes. Leg 1 is written to arrive at the summit by that line.

<!-- b040.report.md -->
# b040 report

## wa_northwest_face_4 (Little Big Chief, NW Face): OMITTED, 0 of 2 written
- DEFECT, two trailheads conflict: waypoint 0 is named "Dingford Creek / Middle Fork Snoqualmie Trailhead" and its pin (47.5172,-121.4544) sits exactly on the Dingford Creek trailhead (47.5174,-121.4542). The `approach` text starts at the Dutch Miller Gap Trailhead (47.5104,-121.3495), which is about 5 more miles up FR 56 and is the start of Trail 1030. The waypoint name also merges "Dingford Creek" and "Middle Fork", which are two separate trailheads about 6 road miles apart. Because the drive and leg 1 both depend on which trailhead is right, I left both blank.
- Note on waypoint 0 refers to "a small steel bridge well short of Dutch Miller Gap", which matches the Dutch Miller Gap trail. That supports the Dutch Miller Gap TH being the correct trailhead and the pin being wrong.
- The route has no camp or bridge waypoint, so leg 1 would have to cover a multi-day approach plus 10 pitches.

## wa_red_mountain_snoqualmie_standard: 1 written (leg 1). Index 0 already had text, so it was left alone.
- Minor issue: the approach says to turn "left (west)" onto #1033. "Left" is right. "West" is doubtful, because the basin trail continues north while the PCT turns east, so I wrote only "bear left".
- Minor issue: the itinerary puts the tarn at "~4.5 mi and ~4,600 ft", but the round trip is only 8 mi, so one-way to the summit is about 4 mi. I left mileage past the junction out.

## wa_sky_mountain_s_route: 6 of 6 written
- DEFECT: approach_logistics.peakLat/peakLng (47.7461,-121.0892) is the Stevens Pass trailhead, not the summit (47.75918,-121.11025).
- Waypoints 3 and 4 are interpolated pins, and their notes say so. I followed the ordering because it matches the prose.
- Possible inconsistency: the approach has the boulder garden "angling southwest" past the lake, while baseFinding then sends you to a gap northwest of the lake. I left the boulder garden out of the legs. The note on waypoint 0 calls the start building "gray/red", while other sources call it red. I wrote "small utility building".

## wa_slippery_slab_tower_ne_face: 1 of 7 written (drive only)
- ORDERING DEFECT, two approaches spliced: the route goes Tunnel Creek TH, then Surprise Lake, then Trap Pass. Surprise Lake belongs to the Surprise Creek approach. Its pin (47.668) is SOUTH of Trap Pass (47.674), so on the Tunnel Creek line a party reaches Trap Pass first and never passes Surprise Lake. distMi 4.7 at Surprise Lake is also impossible from Tunnel Creek. I left all legs blank as the rules require.
- Waypoints 3 to 6 all share one coordinate (47.66888,-121.12634). Waypoint 4 has no elevation.
- Contradictions in the prose: the itinerary says to camp "in the Thunder Mountain Lakes region", but the Trap Lake bivy note says camping there is discouraged. The bivy note calls the tower "a single pitch", while route_breakdown and the topout note say two pitches. The approach says "roughly 10+ miles one-way", but the topout distMi is 7.

## wa_snoqualmie_mountain_the_snostril: 2 of 2 written
- Leg 1 uses the New York Gully / Phantom Slide approach that the row points to, plus the route's start description. Caveat: the first-ascent account says the Snostril lies "much farther west (climber's right)" than the established NW-face lines. The final traverse from the Phantom Slide to the start is therefore not described in detail, and my leg does not invent one.
- Missing data: descent_text, itinerary, and the elevation on both waypoints are null. The waypoint 1 type is lowercase "route".

## wa_south_face_8 (Lundin South Face): 2 of 2 written
- No defects found. The pins agree with the trailhead and summit, and "northwest from the crossing" matches the geometry.
- Gap: approach_logistics is null.

<!-- b041.report.md -->
# b041 report

## wa_south_gully_south_spur — 2/2 written
- 0: Alpental drive (Exit 52, north under I-90, Alpental Road ~1.3 mi to road end; NW Forest Pass; Strasse roads private).
- 1: summer line from Alpental (gully, chockstones force you left, South Spur). The leg then continues over the middle summit with the ~30 m rappel to the north summit.
- Defect: the summit pin (47.442945,-121.409145, 5168) is Guye's listed high point, which is the NORTH summit. The route as described tops out on the SOUTH summit, where the summer version ends. The leg only reaches the pin because it adds the traverse that the itinerary and route_breakdown describe.
- Defect: the primary (winter) approach variant starts at Summit West via Commonwealth, but waypoint 0 is Alpental. I followed the waypoint and the summer variant. The summer variant's own approach from Alpental is vague ("plan a line from public ground"), so leg 1 names no trail.

## wa_south_rib — 2/2 written
- 0: same Alpental drive. 1: Sahale ski track 3/4–1 mi, boulder field and slide alder, traverse east past Blood Sport to the taller talus fan, five pitches. The rib approach was confirmed online.
- Defect: same as above. The rib ends at the south summit and the pin is the north summit, so the leg adds the traverse north with the rappel into the gap. The descent_text instead rappels into the Southeast Couloir, so this is worth a look.

## wa_southwest_face — 2/2 written
- 0: Alpental drive, plus the Alpine Lakes Wilderness permit (from the row's own permit field). 1: Snow Lake Trail, fork toward Source Lake, break off north of the Pineapple Pass gully, four pitches to the summit.
- Note: waypoint 1 distMi is null. The variant gives 3 mi / about 4.8 km, and 4.8 km is 3 mi, so they are consistent.

## wa_spinnaker_peak_s_route — 0 written (whole route omitted)
- Index 0 already has text. All legs 1–6 are left blank: the row splices approaches and contradicts its own pins.
  - The waypoint 0 note, the index-0 directions and `approach` all say to cross US 2 and head SOUTH. But the summit pin (47.77044,-121.13302, which matches Peakbagger) lies about 4 km WEST-NORTHWEST of the Yodelin pin. Spinnaker is north of the highway, northwest of Stevens Pass. The existing index-0 text "heading south" is therefore likely wrong, and I did not overwrite it.
  - `approach_logistics.peakLat/peakLng` (47.745,-121.0933) is a different place from the summit pin. It sits roughly at Stevens Pass itself.
  - `route_breakdown` describes a different approach entirely: the Stevens Pass ski lot, the Skyline Lake road, the PCT northbound, then over Point 5261.
  - Waypoint 4 "Point 5261 Ridge" (the corniced harder variant, per baseFinding) comes before waypoint 5 "South Ridge Gap" at 5,400 ft. baseFinding says the standard line goes straight to the col between 5261 and Spinnaker. That col must be lower than 5,261, so a 5,400 ft gap contradicts it.
  - The itinerary instead describes the basin between Tye Peak and the Spinnaker–Martin ridge, then the ridge.
  - Waypoints 1–5 have no coordinates.

## wa_spire_mountain_scramble — 6/7 written (index 0 already filled)
- Legs 1–6 come from the on-file prose, confirmed online: steep brush-free first 600 ft, cross a logging road, traverse left to the 2,300 ft saddle, hogsback 2,700–3,300 ft, cliff bands 4,200–4,400, traverse SE to about 5,400, NW saddle, class 4-5 step, summit.
- Defects:
  - The existing index-0 text says "No permits are needed", but the `permit` field says a self-issue wilderness permit is required. These conflict.
  - `descent_text` says to return "to the North Fork Skykomish Trail". The route is entirely off-trail and starts from FR 63, so there is no such trail on this route.
  - The saddle is 5,940 ft on the waypoint but 5,920 ft in route_breakdown.
  - The waypoint 1 note says the first 600 ft are "brush-free" while the approach says "blowdown-choked". Both are plausible (downed timber, little brush), but the wording is inconsistent.
  - The trailhead has no coordinates. The approach also says the parking is "past the bridge", while the itinerary says "near the river crossing/hilltop turnaround". These are consistent.

## wa_summit_chief_mountain_south_route — 8/8 written
- 0: the drive was confirmed: Exit 80, Bullfrog Rd 2.8 mi, SR 903 past Cle Elum Lake (16.8 mi total), FR 46 for 4.7 mi, FR 4616 for 1 mi. NW Forest Pass and wilderness permit.
- Legs follow the primary variant: Pete Lake Trail to the fork, right, 0.6 mi, right onto the PCT; switchbacks to Vista Lakes; Pt 5965 to Summit Chief Pass; traverse to the talus basin; snowfield and moat; ramp to the notch; SW gully to the summit.
- Defects:
  - Waypoint 1 "PCT junction near Lemah Meadow" is stored at 4,000 ft with distMi 4.5, and its note describes the Lemah Meadow bypass corner. The actual PCT junction is at about 3,220 ft and about 5.6 mi in (per approach_variants), and 4.5 mi is Pete Lake. The card conflates the junction with the bypass corner. Leg 1 ends at the junction its name gives and states no elevation.
  - The distance to Vista Lakes is inconsistent: the waypoint says 13 mi, the variant sums to about 10 mi, and `approach` says "about 9 miles".
  - Summit Chief Pass is 5,700 ft in the itinerary and 5,900 on the waypoint. The waypoint merges Point 5965 and the pass into one card.
  - The moat waypoint note says "the adjoining gully is often icy". baseFinding places the icy gully BEYOND the notch, and leg 7 follows baseFinding.

<!-- b042.report.md -->
# b042 report

## wa_tailgunner_peak_w_route (8 waypoints, 7 written)
- Written: 0 (drive via FR 6024), 1-5 (creek crossing, rock knob, waterfall ravine, 4,400 ft fork, Tailgunner Pass), 7 (headwall to summit).
- Blank: 6, the gendarmes / cornice headwall leg from the pass. The row contradicts itself about how the summit is reached from the pass: route_breakdown says "crampon up the peak's north slope from the pass", but descent_text and the approach_variant hazards say a cliff band directly north of the pass blocks a direct line. A trip report confirms "north slope, then a relic-cornice headwall on the west ridge". The "minor gendarmes on the crest" exist only in this row.
- DEFECTS:
  - Waypoint 4's note says the brushy weakness at 4,400 ft takes you out of the couloir "to gain the crest of the west ridge". In fact the 4,400 ft fork is a choice between the spring line (straight up Tailgunner Couloir) and the summer line (back left through a brushy weakness in the cliff band), and both lead to Tailgunner Pass. The baseFinding repeats the error ("the way out of the couloir onto ... the crest of the west ridge").
  - Tailgunner Pass is EAST of Tailgunner (Tailgunner lies to the west of the pass, Gunn Peak to the north). The route is called "West Ridge", and the itinerary says "scramble the West Ridge from the pass", but the reported line goes up the north slope to a headwall on the west ridge. The name and the geometry need a look.
  - Pins 3-7 (ravine, weakness, pass, gendarmes, summit) sit on an almost straight NNW line, all marked "estimated". The pass pin is only about 150 m SSE of the summit, with 520 ft of rise between them. They look interpolated, not placed.
  - The start is disputed. The row's approach says to follow the Barclay Lake trail "a short distance" before leaving it, and waypoint 1 is "leave trail" at 0.3 mi. Trip reports say to walk back down the short spur road to the creek. Leg 1 therefore describes only the crossing itself (a logjam about 200 yd upstream of the spur road), not how to get from the trailhead to it.
  - Waypoint 1 has null lat/lng.
  - The itinerary's 3,540 ft gain disagrees with the approach's 4,500-6,000 ft gain in the same row. The summit is given as 5,839 ft in the itinerary and 5,842 ft on the pin.

## wa_the_tooth_fairy (2 waypoints, 2 written)
- 0: the Alpental drive. 1: the approach plus six bolted pitches and the short scramble to the true summit.
- Note: the approach_variant's Fang / backtracking passage is confusing. I left it out and used the agreed line: below Pineapple Pass, traverse west past the Southwest Face to the bolts.

## wa_the_tooth_indentured_servant (2 waypoints, 2 written)
- 0: the drive. 1: the approach, then about 500 ft past the Tooth Fairy to the left side of the big gully, five bolted pitches, and the North Ridge scramble to the summit.
- DEFECTS:
  - Minor conflict: this row says to go "up to Pineapple Pass, then from the pass" west. The Tooth Fairy row says to stay lower and NOT finish at the pass. I wrote "climb toward Pineapple Pass" and did not claim either.
  - permit and approach_logistics are null, although the trailhead is the same as for the other Tooth routes (Alpine Lakes Wilderness permit plus Northwest Forest Pass).
  - The summit pin has distMi null.

## wa_the_tooth_r1 — Northeast Slabs (3 waypoints, 0 written, omitted from JSON)
- Index 0 already had text.
- ORDERING FAULT: the waypoints run trailhead, then "The Tooth summit" (distMi 0), then "Northeast Slabs base". The summit comes before the base of the route, so legs 1-2 were left blank.
- Also: the itinerary says "traverse south into the valley below The Tooth's east face", while the approach describes a talus traverse "around the east side of Source Lake". The route_breakdown has 4 entries, but the itinerary says 5-6 pitches.

## wa_three_queens_middle_peak (drive-only, 1 written)
- 0: Highway 903, then Cooper Lake Road FR 4600 over Cooper Pass to the Mineral Creek trailhead (about 7 mi to the pass plus about 3 mi down). I added no fire-closure claim: FR 4600 and the trail were closed for the 2026 Three Queens Fire, which is a temporary status.
- Small wording defect: approach_logistics.trailheadDirection says "17 miles past Lake Cle Elum". It is 17 miles from Cle Elum, and Lake Cle Elum is passed on the way.

## wa_tooth_chair_traverse (2 waypoints, 2 written)
- 0: the drive. 1: the approach to Pineapple Pass, then the South Face (5.4) to the summit. This is the route's own "historically the South Face" start.
- Note: the array ends at the Tooth summit. The traverse to Chair Peak (Bryant rappel, Chair descent gully) has no waypoints, so the route's actual objective is not pinned.

# b044 report

## wa_accidental_discharge_east_face — 1 of 2 written
- [0] drive written (Suiattle River Road FS 26, about 20 mi, Downey Creek TH; confirmed on the land-manager page). The road is currently washed out, so the text says to check its status instead of making a dated claim.
- [1] left blank. This one leg runs TH to summit, 17.5 mi. The row cannot say how a party from the Downey/Dome-Chickamin side gets onto the east (Blue Glacier) side of the Gunsight crest: the itinerary says "cross the Chickamin/Blue Glacier", the descent rappels "east onto the Blue Glacier", and the Agnes variant says only the eastern approach puts you on the glacier side the route starts from. The crossover could not be confirmed.
- DEFECTS: the wp0 note is pipeline voice ("Corrected - previous waypoints incorrectly placed this route ~59 miles away... a research error"). The wp1 note says its coordinate is "a geometric estimate", which is meta. approach_variants marks the Agnes Creek (High Bridge) variant `primary: true`, but the waypoints, approach and itinerary all use Downey Creek. Do the two approaches disagree about which side of the crest the route starts from?

## wa_around_the_cave_we_go — 2 of 2 written
- [0] drive to Blue Lake TH, SR-20 near MP 161, just west of Washington Pass, NW Forest Pass.
- [1] Blue Lake Trail about 1.5 mi, then the climbers' path left after the small stream, up the Liberty Bell–Concord gully to the north-side notch, then the climb. This agrees with the row's approach ("notch area on Concord Tower's north side").
- No defects. The TH has no elevation or distMi, and the row has no descent.

## wa_bacon_peak_diobsud — 6 of 7 written
- [0] drive: FS 11 to Baker Dam, FS 1106 across the dam, FS 1107, then the signed spur to the road end. Sources say 8 or 9 mi on FS 1107, so the text gives a range. The row's "FR-1107 to its end" leaves out the final spur.
- [1] trail #611 to the wilderness saddle. [2] trail down to Watson Lakes. [4]-[6] written from the row's climbing_route and approach.
- [3] Watson Lakes → Diobsud basin left blank. The row's own approach does NOT go through Watson Lakes: it leaves the trail at the saddle, goes south to Upper Anderson Lake, then crosses the north side of Mt Watson to Diobsud Lakes. A trip report confirms a separate line via Watson Lakes / Watson Glacier / Diobsud saddle, but I could not source its details. So the array follows a different line from the approach prose; it is not spliced, since both reach Diobsud Lakes.
- DEFECTS:
  - The itinerary (all 3 days plus totalNote) describes a different trailhead and route entirely: Bacon Creek Road at about 800 ft, the old roadbed and the southeast ridge past Peak 4800. Its own totalNote admits it is "not the Watson Lakes trailhead used in the approach".
  - The wp1 note puts the Lower Anderson Lake fork at the saddle. The approach puts that fork about 0.5 mi before the saddle.
  - The approach's distances do not add up: 1 mi + about 1 mi + 0.5 mi = about 2.5 mi, yet it says the saddle is at "1.4-1.5 miles".
  - The pins may contradict the prose. climbing_route has the glacier "on the peak's southeast side", heading northwest to a 6,250 ft saddle and then the summit from the southwest. But the wp5 "glacier crossing" pin (6,400 ft) is north-northwest of the summit and higher than that saddle, and the gully pin is southwest of the summit. So leg [6] does not mention the saddle or a bearing.
  - The approach variant describes a non-glacier line to a camp northeast of the summit (a third line).

## wa_beckey_tate — 1 new entry ([1])
- [0] and [2] already had text and were not touched.
- [1] runs to the edge of the talus field at the West Face split, and tells south-face parties to stay on the west side. It does NOT narrate a creek crossing.
- DEFECTS:
  - The existing [2] text starts "From the last belay...". It describes the post-climb scramble to the true summit, not the leg from wp1 (the creek) to the summit. The approach up the basin, the Half Moon col and the six pitches are all missing from it, so the slot is wrong for its position.
  - The wp0 note says "no permit required", but the permit field says a Northwest Forest Pass is needed "to park at developed trailheads". This is a roadside pullout, so it is minor.

## wa_beyond_redlining — 1 of 1 (drive-only)
- [0] drive: Mountain Loop Hwy about 28 mi from Granite Falls (about 17 mi past Verlot), FS 4065 about 2.3 mi to the road end, high clearance, NW Forest Pass. No defects in the slot.

## wa_big_four_mountain_dry_creek_route — 2 of 2 written
- [0] drive: Mountain Loop Hwy about 26 mi from Granite Falls / 14.5 mi past Verlot, NW Forest Pass.
- [1] the full ascent. The order of the couloir and the red slabs is left neutral on purpose (see the defect below).
- DEFECTS:
  - The approach and climbing_route put the red-slab traverse BEFORE the couloir, to gain the "east ridge" below it. The variant's baseFinding puts the couloir first and then a cave, then the red-slab traverse gaining the "South Ridge". These are two different sequences and two different ridges.
  - The descent rappels from "a notch on the south ridge".
  - grade is null, and the rows give no elevation or distMi. The summit pin is 6,170 ft; the commonly cited height is about 6,153 ft (minor).

# b045 report

- [1] Blank on purpose: the route tops out on the summit ridge "well west of the true summit" (route_breakdown and descent_text both say so), and the descent goes northwest from there. The leg would not arrive at the card it names ("Big Four Mountain Summit"). Suggest a "top of couloir / summit ridge" pin instead of, or before, the summit pin.

## wa_big_four_mountain_tower_route — 2 of 2 written

## wa_big_kangaroo_west_face — 2 of 3 written (index 0 already had text)
- [1] TH to the creek crossing, from approach/approach_variants. The pin's distMi 0.4 agrees with the prose's "0.5-0.7 mi" well enough.
- [2] Crossing to the summit via the chockstone gully, three pitches.
- Defect / conflict: approach and approach_variants say the gully leads to the notch "just NORTH of the summit block". The itinerary schedule says to head "toward the crest SOUTH of the summit", and its objective calls the route the "Southwest Rib / West Route". I left the notch's side out of the text. Someone should resolve which side.

## wa_big_snagtooth_west_ridge — 2 of 2 written

- [1] Creek log crossing near the Willow Creek mouth, then the north side of Willow Creek to the basin, 400 ft of scree to the west-ridge saddle, the ridge, then the chimney and slab. This matches the row's prose and an online trip description.

## wa_black_peak_east_buttress — 1 of 2 written

- [1] Blank: the only prose is "accessed from the east side of the Wing Lake basin". The array also has no Heather Pass, Lewis Lake or Wing Lake pins between the TH and the summit, so the TH-to-summit leg would be a 6+ mile multi-stage description with an unknown final line. Nothing online describes the buttress beyond "lower portion easy, then a gully system".
- Defect: discipline "scrambling" with grade null for a named buttress route, and the row has no breakdown, descent or permit. North Cascades NP cross-country camping at Wing Lake needs a backcountry permit, and the row's permit field is null. Consider adding Heather Pass / Wing Lake waypoints.

# b046 report

## wa_black_peak_northeast_ridge — 2/2 written
- [0] drive to Rainy Pass Picnic Area (MP 157, NWFP/interagency/day fee). [1] trail, right fork to Heather Pass, climbers' path to Lewis Lake and Wing Lake, moraine-wide-right line to the NE notch, ridge to summit.
- Defects: none blocking. Only 2 pins on a 2-day route (no Heather Pass / Lewis Lake / Wing Lake camp / notch pins), so leg 1 is the whole approach plus climb.

## wa_blue_s_buttress — 2/2 written
- [0] SR-20 to the hairpin pullout ~1 mi east of Washington Pass. [1] boulder field, cairns, drainage off Poster Peak, stay low at treeline past House Buttress, around the roof's left side onto ledges, crest to summit.
- Possible defect: the on-file approach says the Poster Peak drainage comes in "on the right" heading south toward Kangaroo Pass, but the summit pin lies SSE and slightly EAST of the hairpin pin, so the drainage should be on the left/east of a southbound walker (another online description says "wander SE ... keep looking left (East)"). I avoided left/right in the text; worth checking the prose.
- Permit field says NWFP "at developed trailheads"; the hairpin is a highway pullout, so no pass stated.

## wa_booker_mountain_south_ridge — 2 of 3 written ([0] already had text)
- [1] Cascade Pass Trail 3.7 mi / 1,700 ft to the pass. [2] Sahale Arm toward Sahale Glacier Camp, descend gully near 6,600 ft into Horseshoe Basin, traverse 6,400-6,600 ft past Buckner buttress, snowfield/gully at 7,000-7,200 ft, south ridge to 3rd-class summit gully.
- Oddity (not changed): the prose says leave the trail "near Sahale Camp" (~7,600 ft) yet descend a gully "near 6,600 ft" — implies a 1,000 ft drop not described; the real departure point from Sahale Arm into Horseshoe Basin is probably lower on the arm. Summit distMi 11 looks long (7.3 mi from the pass).

## wa_borrowed_time — 2/2 written
- [0] Darrington, Mountain Loop Hwy 17.2 mi, left on FR 4096 ~1 mi past Bedal CG, rough road to end; unplowed in winter. [1] Trail #705 to the west-face valley ~5,000 ft, central weakness to the flat basin, six pitches to the summit ridge and summit.
- Discrepancy: route `permit` says NWFP at developed trailheads; the trail-association page lists no pass for this trailhead — no pass stated in text. FR 4096 length is given as 2 mi and 3 mi by different sources; text says "to its end" only.
- Pin is Sloan Peak summit while the route tops out on the summit ridge; descent prose implies parties do go to the summit, so leg ends "to the summit ridge and the summit".

## wa_boston_peak_southeast_face — 3 of 5 written ([0] already had text, [4] left blank)
- [1] abandoned road ~1 mi to old mine site, climbers' path, Midas/Morningstar/Boston Creek crossings, into the basin. [2] Quien Sabe left side under Sharkfin Tower, end-run schrund right, up-left to the col. [3] col to summit, written only as what both descriptions share.
- [4] "Cascade Pass" (Junction) after the summit LEFT BLANK — conflict: `descent_text` says reverse to the col then descend Sahale Arm and the Cascade Pass Trail "back to the trailhead" (a different trailhead from the Boston Basin pullout this route starts at), while the `itinerary` says rappel, walk off to Boston Basin camp, and hike out the same boot path. Two records disagree on the descent; the Cascade Pass pin also strands the party ~1.5 mi of road from its car.
- Conflict on the ascent above the col: the `approach`/`climbing_route` say cross the head of the Boston Glacier to SE-side ledges (3rd/4th class, crux below summit ridge), while the approach_variant describes following the connecting ridge, a knife edge, bypassing a false summit east, then ~200 ft of glacier above a schrund to the summit block. Leg 3 states only the shared elements.
- The `approach` says the trailhead is "at the end of Cascade River Road" — wrong; it is a pullout at ~mile 21.7-22, the road end is the Cascade Pass trailhead. Camp pin elev 6,200 vs "high camp ~6,400 ft" in prose.

## wa_boston_peak_west_face — 4/4 written
- [0] Cascade River Road to the Eldorado Creek trailhead at MP 20, winter gate there (land-manager page). [1] road beyond gate to Cascade Pass TH, trail to its highest switchback, angle NE to ~6,400 ft. [2] Soldier Boy drainage, rising traverse to Quien Sabe NW corner, cross to the face, schrund right, lower gully. [3] the five pitches to the true top.
- Defect: the prose puts the Cascade Pass trail's "highest switchback" at ~6,400 ft; Cascade Pass itself is 5,392 ft, so the switchbacks are far lower — the 6,400 ft figure belongs to the angle-point pin above them. Text does not repeat the elevation for the switchback.
- Defect: "West Face route start" pin (48.49555, -121.03709) is ~70 m from the summit pin (48.4954, -121.0362) and essentially at summit height — not at the foot of a five-pitch face. The start pin is misplaced.
- Trailhead pin has no elevation. No summer-approach waypoints exist even though the prose says summer parties use Boston Basin.

# b047 report

## wa_boving_roofs (2/2 written)
- Drive + leg 1 (trail, climbers' path, 3 SW Rib pitches, roofs, finish to summit — matches descent/itinerary that most parties continue to the summit).
- Defect: the two Blue Lake rows disagree on the trailhead's distance from Washington Pass (this row: ~1 mi west; wa_chockstone_route: 1.5 mi west) for the identical pin (48.518965,-120.67436). Online sources also split (1 mi / 1.5 mi). Text says only "just west of Washington Pass".
- Possible defect: trailhead elev 5,400 ft on both rows; common published figure for the Blue Lake TH is ~5,200 ft. Not adjudicated.

## wa_cascade_peak_east_ridge (3 written; index 0 already had text)
- Legs 1-3 written.
- Defect: waypoint 1 "Base of access couloir" has elev 3,700 ft and null lat/lng — it is ABOVE the 3,600 ft trailhead, but the couloir apron is below the road (~3,300 ft per approach_variants; you descend to the river). Elevation wrong, pin missing.
- Defect: route_breakdown and itinerary disagree on pitch order — breakdown: crux crack -> class 4 crest -> final "NW Chimney" summit tower; itinerary: traverse to NW Chimney in the moat, crux crack, then 5.8 chimney, then loose pitch/simul. Leg 3 states only what both share (loose crux crack first, loose route-finding to summit).
- Note: approach says couloir ~3,000 ft, itinerary 3,400 ft; col is ~6,760 ft (pin 6,800 fine). Used 3,400.

## wa_castle_in_the_sky (1/2 written: drive only)
- Leg 1 left blank: the route (P12) ends on Whatcom's WEST summit, but the northeast summit is the higher one, and the summit pin (48.8576,-121.3735) sits ~650 m east of the commonly published peak coordinate, i.e. likely the NE/true summit. A leg ending at "Whatcom Peak summit" would not be the route's end. Also no intermediate waypoints for a 3-day approach (Hannegan Pass, Chilliwack ford, Easy Ridge/Easy Peak bivy, Imperfect Impasse, Perfect Pass) — the single TH->summit leg covers ~20 miles.
- Defect: approach_logistics, permit and season are null; descent_text says "from the west summit", consistent with the route ending there, not at the pinned summit.

## wa_chockstone_route (4/4 written)
- Defect: "Chockstone crux" pin (48.5133,-120.6553) lies NORTH of the NEWS summit pin (48.51291) — the gully is between NEWS and SEWS (SEWS pin 48.51217, south), so the crux should be south of the NEWS summit. Pin is on the wrong side. Leg text follows the gully and states no compass direction.
- Waypoint 1 has null lat/lng. Leg 1 distance written as "a little over a mile" because this row says 1.5 mi and wa_boving_roofs says 1.25 mi to the same climbers' path junction.
- approach_variants names a "signed Liberty Bell Trail #727" junction — unverified; not used.

## wa_cinderella_peak_scramble (1 written; 5 already had text)
- Leg 2 (split -> Green Creek crossing) written.
- Defect/conflict: itinerary says FS-38 is closed by a washout at milepost 7 (~4 mi short) and to bike; existing index 0 text says the road is normally drivable to the end. Dated status either way — worth reconciling.
- Minor: existing index 1 text says the trail reaches Hildebrand Creek "near 2,600 ft"; pin is 2,700.

## wa_clean_break (2/2 written)
- Drive + leg 1 (approach + 15 pitches to summit; descent starts "from the summit", so leg arrives at the named point).
- Minor: Silver Star Creek crossing "milepost 171" with WA Pass at ~MP 162 + 8.5 mi ~= MP 170.5; consistent enough.

# b048 report

## wa_colfax_peak_fords_theatre (drive-only, 1 waypoint): 1/1 written
- Index 0: the drive from SR 542 via Glacier Creek Road FS 39, about 8 mi, to the Heliotrope Ridge TH. The TH pin (48.8022, -121.8947) is on the correct north-side trailhead.

## wa_colfax_peak_kimchi_suicide_volcano (drive-only, 1 waypoint): 1/1 written
- Same drive as Ford's Theatre. The pin is correct.

## wa_colfax_peak_polish_route (drive-only, 8 waypoints): 1/8 written
- Index 0: drive. Indexes 1-7 are blank because the row is drive-only.
- DEFECT: idx 3 (Coleman Glacier toward the north face, 48.7572), idx 4 (bergschrund, 48.7514), idx 5 (AI4+ pitch, 48.7559) and idx 6 (WI6 crux, 48.7591) all sit SOUTH of the Colfax summit (48.7715). The north face and the Coleman approach are north and northwest of the summit, so these pins are on the wrong side of the peak, and their order runs south-to-north. They look like the copied Schriebers-side pins.
- DEFECT (minor): idx 0's pin (48.80223, -121.895575) differs slightly from approach_logistics' trailheadLat/Lng (48.80218, -121.894725). The gap is about 60 m and harmless.
- DEFECT (minor): idx 1 has lat/lng null. Its note, "climbers' path right at the Kulshan Cabin site junction", matches the land-manager and trail descriptions.
- DEFECT (minor): descent_text opens with "No first-hand account describes a rappel descent...". That is meta prose about sources rather than written for a climber.

## wa_complete_south_buttress (3 waypoints): 1 written (idx 2)
- Idx 0 already had text and was not touched.
- Idx 1 is blank. DEFECT: the pin named "Complete South Buttress base / area reference point" (48.52623, -120.70347) sits about 30 m from the summit pin (48.526495, -120.703655). It is on the summit, not at the toe of the lower ridge near 6,500 ft. A leg to the "base" would contradict its pin. Its elevation is also missing.
- Idx 2: the climb from the toe to the summit, taken from route_breakdown.

## wa_concerto_in_c_for_drill_and_hammer (3 waypoints): 2 written (idx 1, 2)
- Idx 0 already had text.
- Idx 1: the approach to the grassy saddle: the cut logs and cable, turn right, ford Squire Creek, go downstream, a tributary, two gullies. This is confirmed by the area's published approach. Direction is consistent with the pins: the saddle is SW of the TH, and right = west when heading up-valley.
- Idx 2: from the saddle down ramps to the P1 anchor, then 14 pitches to the summit chain anchor. The route is described as ending at the summit chain anchor on the summit crest.
- DEFECT: the row's `approach` field describes the Waterfall Basin approach: the streambed/alder brush and "open basin roughly 2 hours". The South Face approach is different: the cut logs, the grassy saddle, about 3 hr. approach_variants has it right.
- DEFECT: idx 2 (the summit) has lat/lng null, and its elevation of 4,958 ft labelled "true summit/high point" is unverified. The route tops out on the "summit crest" above Buckeye Basin.
- Note: idx 0's own drive already handles the parking ambiguity (washout vs the end of the road).

## wa_crater_mountain_standard_route (2 waypoints): 2/2 written
- Idx 0: SR 20 to the Canyon Creek TH, MP 141, 32.8 mi E of Marblemount / 55.1 mi W of Winthrop. A NW Forest Pass or the day fee is required, and the Pasayten permit is free. The TH pin matches the land-manager coordinate exactly (48.7065, -120.9183).
- Idx 1: the whole ascent: #738, the Canyon Creek ford, the junction near 5,000 ft, LEFT on #746 to Crater Lake, the SE ridge, the gully near 7,600 ft (traverse across rather than following the paint up), and trail for the last quarter mile.
- DEFECT (minor): route_breakdown and descent_text say the trail fords "Granite Creek" as well. The land manager mentions only the unbridged Canyon Creek crossing, so I left Granite Creek out.
- DEFECT (minor): approach_logistics.trailheadLat/Lng (48.7063, -120.9185) differs slightly from the idx 0 pin (48.7065, -120.9183).
- Inconsistency: the junction elevation is given as 5,000-5,260 ft, and the Crater Lake basin as "~5,000-5,700 ft". I wrote "near 5,000 ft", which matches the land manager.

# b051 report

## wa_direct_west_face (Pernod Spire): 2 of 2 written
- Drive: SR 20 pullout near MP 166, about 0.7 mi west of the Cutthroat Lake Rd turnoff (Cutthroat Rd is near MP 167).
- Leg 1: boot path, Early Winters Creek crossing, bench camp, then up and right past two buttresses into the gully, then 8 pitches.
- DEFECT: the summit pin (48.5526, -120.5903) is identical to the Chablis Spire summit pin on wa_east_face_2 (48.55259, -120.59032). They are two different spires (they share the Pernod-Chablis notch), so at most one of them is right.
- DEFECT (minor): the prose puts the pullout "roughly 3.75 miles east of the Washington Pass hairpin", but wa_east_face_2's trailheadDirection gives "about 2.8 miles" for the same pullout. I left the hairpin distance out of both drives.
- Itinerary day 2 says the descent is "a multi-pitch rappel down the standard line before hiking back out past the bench". descent_text says something different: rappel east to the neve, then climb back up to Burgundy Col. The itinerary is vague and probably wrong about the route.
- The itinerary has day-1 gain at 1,100 ft, but the approach climbs about 2,400 ft from the creek to the bench.

## wa_dolphin_chimney (South Early Winters Spire): 2 of 2 written
- Drive: SR 20 to the Blue Lake Trailhead, south side, about 1 mi west of Washington Pass, NW Forest Pass.
- Leg 1: Blue Lake Trail, climbers' path, Y-larch, SW Rib pitches, Dolphin, finish to the summit. Most parties finish to the summit (per descent_text), so the leg ends where the card does.
- No defects found.

## wa_doorway_flake (Squire Creek Wall South Face): 1 of 3 written
- Index 0 already had text, so it was left "".
- Leg 1 (grassy saddle) is written from the approach_variant. A credible online description of the South Face approach confirms it: Trail #654 sign, 8-10 min to the cut logs and cable, turn right, ford, two gullies, saddle, 3 hrs.
- Index 2 (summit) left "". The pin has null lat/lng and sits at 4,958 ft, which is about 1,700 ft above the 3,249 ft saddle. The 5-pitch route ends at a chain anchor and is rappelled, so I cannot confirm it reaches the wall's high point.
- DEFECT: the `approach` prose was copied from the Waterfall Basin description. It says you break into "the open basin roughly 2 hours from the car" and that South Face is a wall "within that same basin". That contradicts the variant: South Face is reached at the grassy saddle in about 3 hours.
- DEFECT: peakLat/peakLng in approach_logistics are the saddle coordinates, not a summit.
- Possible issue: route_breakdown puts the flake "at the nearest point of the far western wall". That may mean the route starts from the smaller saddle 10 min west rather than the main saddle. Unverified.

## wa_dorado_needle_east_ridge: 2 of 3 written (index 0 already had text)
- Leg 1: Eldorado climbers' path, Eldorado Glacier, high camp, Inspiration and McAllister glaciers to the col.
- Leg 2: the East Ridge climb from the col to the summit, from route_breakdown.
- DEFECT (important): the waypoint 1 pin "Col between Pt. 8386 and Dorado Needle" is at 48.53755, -121.13436. That is effectively the summit of Eldorado Peak (about 48.5376, -121.1340, 8,868 ft), not a 7,600 ft col beside the Needle. The pin needs moving to the actual col near Dorado Needle. My text describes the named col, and the map line will be wrong until the pin is fixed. If you would rather not ship text over a known-bad pin, blank legs 1 and 2.
- The waypoint 1 pin also has no elevation.

## wa_east_face (Middle Gunsight): 2 of 2 written
- Drive: SR 530 north about 8 mi from Darrington, then the Suiattle River Rd (FS 26) east about 20 mi to the Downey Creek TH, NW Forest Pass. Note: the land manager currently reports the Suiattle River Road impassable after December 2025 washouts. I kept that dated status out of the text, but the drive is not currently usable.
- Leg 1: Downey Creek Trail, Bachelor Creek, Cub Lake and Itswoot Ridge, Dome Glacier, Dome-Chickamin col base camp, Chickamin Glacier, then the climb.
- Possible issue: route_breakdown says the twin cracks are "visible from the Blue Glacier" (east side), while the approach and descent use the Chickamin Glacier. That fits the alternative start, which turns the corner from the moat at the Middle-South notch, but it is worth a check.
- The note calls the 17 mi distance approximate.

## wa_east_face_2 (Chablis Spire): 2 of 2 written
- Drive: same pullout as Pernod.
- Leg 1: bench camp, Burgundy Col, east side down 200-300 ft, traverse south to Chablis, then 4 pitches.
- DEFECT: the summit pin is identical to Pernod's summit pin (see above).
- DEFECT: the trailhead pin (48.549, -120.630965) is about 450 m north of Pernod's pin for the same pullout (48.545, -120.629). Minor, but they should be one point.
- The `permit` field says a NW Forest Pass is needed "at developed trailheads". Pernod's row says none is needed at this roadside pullout. I used the Pernod statement for both rows.

<!-- b052.report.md -->
# b052 report

## wa_east_mcmillan_spire_west_ridge — 0 written (omitted from JSON)
Index 0 already filled. Legs 1-6 left blank: **ordering fault**. Waypoint 3 "Basin notch above camp" (6,200 ft) is placed AFTER Terror Basin Camp (5,800 ft), and its pin sits north of camp. Independent trip reports put the 6,200-ft notch on the APPROACH: you traverse north from the heather benches to the notch (first view into Terror Basin, and of Azure Lake off the east rim), then descend loose rock into the basin. The row's own fields agree (itinerary: "then descends slightly into Terror Basin"; Inspiration row: camp "just below" the 6,000-6,200 ft saddle). From camp the McMillan line makes a descending traverse to the glacial lake outlet ~5,500 ft, not a climb to a notch. The notch's note ("from here the route crosses basin slabs ... toward the col") tells a climber the wrong thing.
Other defects: itinerary day 2 has the party summit WEST McMillan first and traverse the ridge east (with a rappel), while climbing_route/baseFinding go from the West-East col straight to the East summit — two different lines. Trailhead pin (48.6828,-121.2693) differs ~450 m from the approach_logistics trailhead (48.68664,-121.27121) used by the other Goodell routes.

## wa_east_ridge_2 (Snowking East Ridge) — 2 of 3 written
Index 0 already filled. Leg 1 avoids naming left/right at the Point 5116 saddle (describes the branch that climbs to Point 5791).
Defects: primary variant's baseFinding says "If you find yourself dropping toward a lake ... you have taken the other branch and are on the Kindy Ridge line that serves the standard route" — but this route's own itinerary and descent_text say it uses the Kindy Ridge approach, camps at Cyclone Lake, and trip reports confirm the Kindy Ridge -> Pt 5791 -> above Cyclone Lake -> east ridge line IS this route. That paragraph contradicts the row. Summit pin elev 7,400 vs 7,433 ft in the itinerary.

## wa_east_ridge_3 (Silver Star East Ridge) — 1 of 2 written (index 0 only)
**Array fault**: index 0 is the Hwy 20 MP 166 pullout where the Burgundy Col DESCENT finishes, not the Cedar Creek Trailhead where the climb starts; the start trailhead is absent. Leg 1 (pullout -> summit) would be the descent reversed, so left blank. Index 0 written as the drive to that pullout, framed as the shuttle/descent car.

## wa_east_ridge_4 (Inspiration East Ridge) — 2 of 2 written
Defect: trailhead pin elev 500 ft vs 600 ft in approach_logistics and every other Goodell row.

## wa_east_slope (Primus) — 1 written (index 2)
Only index 2 was empty. Defects: Borealis bivy (index 3) has null lat/lng. Existing leg 1 carries a dated "bridge reported out in past years" claim. Existing leg 3 says the path climbs "the ridge dividing McAllister and Thunder creeks" after crossing Thunder Creek, while the Borealis/Primus pins lie west of Thunder Creek — worth a check.

## wa_east_twin_needle_south_route — 6 of 6 written
**Pin defect**: Eye Col pin (48.7601,-121.2863) is ~2.9 km SE of the East Twin Needle summit pin (48.7767,-121.3115, which matches published coordinates), yet the col is the notch immediately beside the summit. The Eye Col pin is wrong (likely near Mount Terror); the camp -> col pin bearing (east) is therefore wrong too, so leg 4 avoids compass words. Summit elev 7,840 vs ~7,868 ft published. Note also: climbing_route starts from a basin camp below Mount Terror (~6,050 ft), not the Chopping Block saddle camp waypoint; leg 4 is written from the saddle camp using the approach's "drop ~200 ft into Crescent Creek Basin" text.

## Drive (Goodell routes)
Written as: SR-20 to Newhalem, first left after crossing Goodell Creek, immediately left at the T, uphill to Upper Goodell Creek Group Camp (~600 ft); path starts just down the road. McMillan's existing index 0 ("park at Goodell Creek Group Campground, path behind a large boulder") is consistent.

# b053 report

## wa_east_wilmans_spire: 3 of 3 written
- Index 0 is the drive from Granite Falls to Barlow Pass. Index 1 is the gated road to Monte Cristo (the Twin Bridges washout has been there a long time). Index 2 runs from the Glacier Basin Trail up the gully, around to the east notch, and up the 3 pitches to the summit.
- Possible defect: the variant prose follows the route page in saying the spire is "on your left" from the Glacier Basin Trail. Measured from the pins, the spire sits marginally to the RIGHT of a straight townsite-to-basin line, so I left left/right out. The trail's switchbacks may explain it, so this is not proven wrong.
- The trailhead pin (48.027,-121.4447) and approach_logistics (48.02639,-121.44389) agree to within about 70 m.

## wa_easy_getaway: 1 of 3 written (index 0 already had text)
- Index 1 is written: trail #483, then the climbers' path to the slab platform.
- Index 2 is left blank. DEFECT: waypoint 2 "Cutthroat Wall" (Crag) sits on the same coordinates as waypoint 1 "Cutthroat Wall Base" (48.5458,-120.6479 against -120.64786), so the leg has no length. Waypoint 1 is also typed "Topout" even though it is the base of the wall. Waypoint 2 has no elevation.

## wa_eldorado_peak_east_ridge: 3 of 4 written (index 0 already had text)
- DEFECT in the existing index 0 text: it says Cascade River Road runs "to its end at the Eldorado trailhead". The Eldorado lot is a small pullout at about mile 20. The road carries on about 3 more miles to the Cascade Pass trailhead, so the Eldorado lot is not the end.
- Index 0 pin (48.494,-121.121, 2160 ft) disagrees with approach_logistics (48.49261,-121.11761, about 2,100 ft) by about 300 m. The north_ridge and northeast_face rows use the logistics coordinate.
- The camp pin (48.538,-121.118) is not the same as the north_ridge row's "East Ridge camp" (48.533,-121.129). Both rows describe the same high camp, about 1 km apart.
- Index 2 "Inspiration Glacier" (8200 ft) is a vague name for a point. I wrote the leg as crossing the glacier to the Gap and passing north through it.

## wa_eldorado_peak_eldorado_glacier_nw: 3 of 4 written (index 0 already had text)
- The existing index 0 has the same "to its end" error as the East Ridge row.
- Leg 2 (camp to the couloir base) uses the notch-and-rappel entry into the Marble Creek cirque. The variant prose and a trip report agree on it.

  - The itinerary says the couloir is "2-3 long pitches". climbing_route says "eight 60 m pitches", about 1,000 ft.
  - Day 2 of the itinerary descends "over the pass near Dorado Needle back to camp", which contradicts descent_text (East Ridge walk-off).

  - descent_text says the entry rappel is "40-meter... off the Inspiration Glacier side". The variant prose says a full 60 m gets you through in one, or a 35 m downclimb plus two 30 m rappels. The figures are inconsistent.

- Index 0 (the drive), index 1 (trailhead to camp) and index 3 (crest to summit) are written.
- Index 2 (camp to the base of the North Ridge) is left blank because of a CONFLICT:
  - The approach text says "descend toward the NW Ice Couloir notch", and the variant says "the route goes down before it goes up... descend the Inspiration Glacier". But camp is at 7,600 ft and the notch is given as about 8,100 ft, so reaching it means going up.
  - The NW Couloir row says to follow the East Ridge track UP to about 8,100 ft.

<!-- b054.report.md -->
# b054 report

## wa_eldorado_peak_west_arete — 2 of 2 written
- Index 0: the drive (Marblemount, Cascade River Road, about 20 miles, gravel from about mile 10, parking at 2,100 ft). Index 1: the trailhead-to-summit leg.
- DEFECT (conflict): the on-file fields disagree about how a party gets down to the base. `approach` says to go to the Eldorado/Triad notch and descend the glacier on its northwest side. `approach_variants` and `itinerary` say to cross to the Dorado Needle Col / "Dorado Col" and downclimb loose 4th class into the Marble Creek cirque. These are different cols: The Triad is SW of Eldorado and Dorado Needle is N. The leg says only what they agree on ("descend west into the Marble Creek cirque") and names neither col.
- DEFECT: waypoint 0's `note` names a third party as its source ("Timberline Mountain Guides' route description explicitly starts..."). That breaks the no-sources rule.
- DEFECT: the array has only TH and summit. There is no high camp (about 7,400 ft) and no base-of-arete pin, so one leg has to cover a 3-day approach plus the climb.

## wa_elephant_butte_standard_route — 2 of 2 written
- Index 0: the drive to Diablo (SR-20 MP 126, cross the Stetattle Creek bridge, bear right, park across from the pool). Index 1: the trail, the unmarked junction, the ridge crest, the high point, the saddle, the summit. The pins agree (summit is NW of Diablo).
- Minor: `approach` puts the Sourdough Creek crossing at "around mile 4", before a junction at 4,300-4,400 ft. On the lookout trail that elevation comes much earlier than mile 4, so the order and mileage are doubtful. The leg leaves the creek crossing out.
- Minor: the bypass side at the Butte is unrecorded (the row says so itself), so the leg does not name one.

## wa_ellation — 1 of 4 written (drive only)
- Index 0: the drive (SR-542, 12.5 mi past Glacier, FR-32 for 5.3 mi to the road end, NW Forest Pass).
- ORDERING FAULT, legs 1-3 left blank: the order is TH → "Top of Ellation" (Topout) → "crag (Ellation start)" → "Mamie Peak (top of buttress)" Summit, so the topout comes before the start. All three non-TH pins share one coordinate (48.91508, -121.58401). "Topout" and "Summit" duplicate each other: both are 5,000 ft and both are the top of the 8-pitch buttress, not a summit of Mamie Peak.
- DEFECT: `approach` says FR-32 runs "about 4 miles" to the trailhead. `approach_logistics` and the land manager say 5.3 mi.
- DEFECT: the TH pin (-121.592) differs from `approach_logistics.trailheadLng` (-121.5894), about 200 m apart.
- DEFECT: `approach` text is pipeline voice ("This crag's coordinates correspond to...", "published approach details ... are sparse and inconsistent").

## wa_ellen_pea — 2 of 3 written (index 0 already had text)
- Leg 1: TH to the upper ledge, by the gully and past the lower ledge. Leg 2: walk right along the upper ledge to the start beneath the cave, with the 40 m corner and twin cracks as the check.
- DEFECT (pins vs prose): the prose says "head directly uphill (north)", and so does the TH note. The pins put the wall WEST of the TH: TH -120.6310 vs wall -120.6398, at nearly the same latitude. The ground agrees with the pins: the highway runs N-S near -120.631 at about 4,300 ft, ground at the wall pin is 6,097 ft, and terrain rises to the west. The leg gives no compass direction.
- DEFECT: the existing index-0 text (and `approach`) says the pullout is "about 3 miles east of the hairpin turns and a similar distance from the Washington Pass summit". The hairpin is just below the pass, so this is muddled. The index-0 text also reasons from wall size to "the parking is the trailhead", which reads oddly.
- DEFECT: waypoints 1 and 2 share one coordinate and one elevation (5,895 ft; ground at that point is about 6,097). Waypoint 1 is typed "Junction" but its note describes the route.
- Minor: the descent prose says "two ropes" and "about an hour". The itinerary says "2-3+ hours" of rappelling. These contradict each other.

## wa_excavation_aka_the_crack — 2 of 3 written (index 0 already had text)
- The order TH → topout → base matches how this crag is actually used: walk around to the top, rig a top-rope from the tree, lower to the base. So I wrote both legs. All three pins share one coordinate. If the order was only a template, legs 1-2 may need review.
- DEFECT: elevation. The TH and topout are stored at 1,000 ft. Ground at the pin is 2,036 ft. The existing index-0 directions repeat the wrong "about 1,000 feet" to the climber.
- The direction to walk around (left or right side) is not recorded, so the leg names no side.

## wa_first_amendment — 2 of 3 written (index 0 already had text)
- Leg 1: creek crossing, base of Spontaneity Arete, the 250-yard ledge traverse, the 100 ft chimney start, then the technical pitches to the join. Leg 2: Spontaneity's last 1.5 pitches by the Goldie Crack to the summit.
- DEFECT: "Upper Mountain" (Topout) and "summit" share one coordinate and one elevation (6,386 ft). Ground at that pin is about 6,693 ft. Stored TH elevation is 4,900 ft, but ground at the TH pin is about 4,704 ft.
- DEFECT: `descent_text` is pipeline voice ("The original route description does not specify a descent; ... parties most likely descend...").
- Minor: the permit field mentions a Northwest Forest Pass "at developed trailheads". This trailhead is a highway turnout.
